## Question 1(a) (Year: 2024 | Paper: GS IV)
**Question ID: 2024-gs4-q1-unacademy**

**Q1.(a) The application of Artificial Intelligence as a dependable source of input for administrative rational decision-making is a debatable issue. Critically examine the statement from the ethical point of view. (150 Words, 10 Marks)** ### Answer **Answer:** **Introduction** : **Artificial Intelligence (AI)** is an **emerging technology** that enables computers and machines to simulate human intelligence and problem-solving capabilities. The integration of Artificial Intelligence (AI) in administrative decision-making has emerged as a pivotal development in public administration.On the one hand, Artificial intelligence promises enhanced efficiency and data-driven insights, similarly it also raisessignificantethical,social,andpracticalconcerns, making its application a debatable issue. **Body:** **Advantages of application of Artificial intelligence as** **a dependable source of input for rational decision** ## **making in administration** 1. Artificial intelligence systems can minimise human biases in the decision making process, leading to more consistent outcomes. This is particularly important in areas like public policy where equitable treatment is crucial.

2. Artificial intelligence can process large amounts of information quickly, enabling faster decision making. This efficiency can streamline public services , reduce bureaucratic delays and allocate resources more effectively. **Ethical Concerns related to application of Artificial** ## **Intelligence ( AI) in administrative decision making** - **Job loss:** The automation of administrative functions may lead to job losses, particularly in roles traditionally held by humans. This raises ethical questions about the societal impact of AI deployment

- **Bias in AI algorithms:** AI systems can perpetuate existing biases present in training data. This can lead to discriminatory practices in areas like hiring, law enforcement, and welfare distribution, undermining fairness and equality.

- **Artificial** **intelligence** **lacks** **essential** **human qualities** like emotional intelligence, innovation,creativity and empathy which may impact the overall outcome of best administrative decision making.

- **Lack of accountability :** Artificial Intelligence technologies can be misused for malicious purposes like privacy breaches or cyber-attacks which may impact the decision making of an administrator. For example, Deep Flakes for spreading misinformation and propaganda which may disturb the social harmony. **Conclusion:** While Artificial intelligence holds potential for improved efficiency and objectivity, it simultaneously raises concerns regarding bias, transparency, and the human elements essential to governance. Striking a balance between leveraging Artificial intelligence’s capabilitiesandensuringethicaloversightisimperative for fostering public trust and enhancing the legitimacy ofadministrativedecisions.Assuch,acritical,nuanced approach is essential in navigating the challenges posed by AI in the realm of public administration.

## Question 2(b) (Year: 2024 | Paper: GS IV) **Q1.(b) “Ethics encompasses several key dimensions that are crucial in guiding individuals and organizations towards morally responsible behaviour.” Explain the key dimensions of ethics that influence human actions. Discuss how these dimensions shape ethical decision-making in the professional context. (150 Words, 10 Marks)** ### Answer **Answer:** **Introduction:** Ethics in human actions is a **crucial aspect of moral** **philosophythatdealswiththerightnessandwrongness** of actions. It involves analyzing and evaluating actions based on various ethical theories and principles to determine whether they are morally acceptable or not. Ethics encompasses various dimensions that **guide** **human behavior and ethical decision-making.** **Key dimensions of ethics that influence human** ## **actions** 1. **Virtue Ethics:** It is believed that human beings could develop virtues through practice, which would enable them to live a good life. This approach centers on the character and virtues of the individual making the decision. It stresses the importance of personal integrity, honesty, and responsibility. In a professional context, a leader guided by virtue ethics would focus on fostering a positive organizational culture and role-modeling ethical behavior.
**Question ID: 2024-gs4-q2-unacademy**

2. **Justice and Fairness:** This dimension emphasizes equity and impartiality in ethical decisionmaking. It advocates for fairness in treatment and consideration of the rights and needs of all stakeholders. In professional settings, this could involveensuringfairhiringpracticesandequitable distribution of resources.

3. **Ethics of loveand** care:Thisperspective highlights the importance of relationships and caring for others. It emphasizes empathy and compassion in decision-making. In a workplace, this may manifest in supportive policies that prioritize employee well-being.

4. **Deontology:Thisperspectiveprioritizesadherence** to rules and duties. Actions are considered ethical if they comply with established moral principles, regardless of the consequences. For example, a public servant might refuse to engage in corrupt practices even if it could yield short-term benefits for themselves or their organization. **Dimensions that shapes the ethical decision-making** **in the professional context:** **Professional ethics** is an important aspect of ethics in human actions. It involves the application of ethical principles and values in various professions, such as medicine, law, business, and engineering. Ethical conduct in these professions is crucial for maintaining public trust and ensuring that professionals act in the best interest of their clients or patients. The following dimensions significantly shape ethical decisionmaking in professional settings through the following

## **mechanisms** 1. **Stakeholder Consideration:** Ethical decisionmakingintheworkplaceofteninvolvesconsidering the interests and rights of various stakeholders. Justice and fairness compel professionals to address the implications of their decisions on employees, clients, and the community, thereby fostering a more inclusive approach.

2. **PolicyDevelopment:Organizationsoftenestablish** codes of ethics or conduct that reflect these dimensions. These policies guide employees in making decisions aligned with the organization’s ethicalstandards,promotingconsistencyinethical behavior across the board.

3. **Organizational Culture:** The prevailing ethical dimension within an organization influences its culture. An organization that values virtue ethics may cultivate an environment of trust and integrity, leading employees to prioritize ethical behavior and accountability.

4. **Conflict Resolution:** In situations of ethical conflict, these dimensions provide a basis for resolving disputes. For instance, care ethics might guide a manager to mediate conflicts with empathy, while deontological principles could drive adherence to contractual obligations. **Conclusion:** Grasping the essential dimensions of ethics is vital for professionals as they tackle intricate decision-making challenges. By integrating virtue ethics, justice and fairness, ethics of love and care, and deontological approachintotheirpractices,professionalscanenhance their ethical awareness and foster an environment that prioritizes integrity and responsibility. This not only leads to sound decision-making but also cultivates trust and respect among stakeholders, ultimately contributing to sustainable organizational success.

## Question 3(a) (Year: 2024 | Paper: GS IV) **Q2.(a) “It is not enough to talk about peace, one must believe in it; and it is not enough to believe in it, one must act upon it.” In the present context, the majorweaponindustriesofthedevelopednations are adversely influencing continuation of number of wars for their own self-interest, all around the world. What are the ethical considerations of the powerful nations in today’s international arena to stop continuation of ongoing conflicts? (150 Words, 10 Marks)** ### Answer **Answer:** **Introduction:** It is not enough to talk about peace, one must believe in it; and it is not enough to believe in it, one must act upon it.” In today’s world, the influence of major weapon industries from developed nations is a significant factor in the continuation of numerous conflicts. These industries often prioritize their own **economicandpoliticalinterestsoverglobalpeaceand** **stability.** This situation raises **critical ethical questions** about the **responsibilities of powerful nations** in **promoting peace** and **preventing war.** - For instance, the ongoing **conflict between Russia** **and Ukraine** has seen significant involvement from weapon industries, with various countries supplying arms that fuel the war.
**Question ID: 2024-gs4-q3-unacademy**

- Similarly,theconflictbetweenIsraelandPalestine isexacerbatedbytheflowofweaponsfromexternal powers, further destabilizing the region.

## **Body** ## **1. Prioritizing Peace Over Profit** - Whileeconomicincentivesmayencouragethesale of arms, ethical behavior demands that the profit motive should not overshadow the promotion of peace.

- For instance, the Saudi-led intervention in Yemen has seen continued arms sales from Western nations, despite widespread human rights violations and the humanitarian crisis.

- Ethical governance requires that leaders acknowledge the moral consequences of profiting from conflict and take steps to regulate or reduce the export of weapons to conflict zones.

## **2. Responsibility to Protect (R2P)** - **Nations** have a moral **obligation to protect** **populations from** mass atrocities, **such as** **genocide, war crimes, ethnic cleansing, and** **crimes against humanity.** - They should be working through **diplomacy,** **sanctions, and humanitarian interventions to end** violence and protect vulnerable populations. **3. Promotion of Diplomacy and Peaceful** ## **Resolutions** - Prioritizing diplomatic efforts and peaceful negotiations over military interventions.

- This involves supporting international organizations like the **United Nations in conflict** **resolution and peacekeeping missions.** ## **4. Regulation of Arms Trade** - **Implementing strict regulations** on the **export of** **arms** to conflict zones.

- This includes adhering to international treaties like the **Arms Trade Treaty (ATT),** which aims to prevent and eradicate illicit trade in conventional arms.

## **5. Economic and Humanitarian Support** - Providing economic aid and humanitarian assistance to conflict-affected regions to address the root causes of **conflict, such as poverty,** **inequality, and lack of education.** - India releases **$2.5 million in aid** for **Palestine** **refugees.** ## **6. Ethical Business Practices** - Encouraging weapon industries to adopt **ethical** **business practices.Ethical considerations** require transparency in the global arms trade. Nations must ensure that their arms sales are **legal,** **transparent, and compliant with international** **law,** ## **7. Support for Disarmament Initiatives** - Actively participating in and supporting global **disarmament initiatives to reduce the** **proliferation of weapons** and promote a culture of peace.

## **Challenges and Realities** - While these ethical considerations are crucial, powerfulnationsoftenfacechallengesinbalancing their **national interests with global ethical** **responsibilities.** - The influence of **powerful weapon industries,** **geopolitical interests, and economic benefits** fromarmstradecancomplicateeffortstopromote **peace.** **Conclusion:** To genuinely act upon the belief in peace, powerful nations must commit to ethical governance, prioritize human rights, and collaborate with international communities to create a more peaceful world. This involves not only policy changes but also a **shift in** **mindset towards valuing human life and dignity** **above economic and political gains.** ---

## Question 2(b) (Year: 2024 | Paper: GS IV) **(b) Global warming and climate change are the outcomes of human greed in the name of development, indicating the direction in which extinction of organisms including human beings is heading towards loss of life on Earth. How do you put an end to this to protect life and bring equilibrium between the society and the environment? (150 Words, 10 Marks)** ### Answer

## **Answer** ## **Introduction** - Global warming and climate change, driven by human greed in the name of development, are accelerating the planet’s path toward **ecological** **disaster and mass extinction (6th mass extinction** . **currently going on)** - Human greed, driven by overconsumption and materialism, has exacerbated environmental degradation.Throwawaycultureandconsumerism worsentheclimatecrisis.Therelationshipbetween society and the environment must shift from one of exploitation to one of balance and harmony.

- Unchecked industrialization and materialism, reminiscent of the myth of **Bhasmasura** in Hindu mythology, could **lead to self-destruction.** Just as Bhasmasura sought to destroy with his own powers, our relentless pursuit of economic growth is threatening our existence. **Body:** To put end to this to protect life and bring equilibrium between the society and the environment, following

## **way can be helpful** - One of the core solutions lies in embracing the EthicalResponsibilityandSustainableStewardship, where humanity views itself as caretakers of the Earth rather than its masters.

- Adopting the principle of intergenerational justice in all public policy that emphasizes our duty to preserve the planet’s resources for future generations is vital.

- Climate action requires involvement from individuals, families, and communities, not just governments (As India’s ‘Lifestyle for the Environment’ (LiFE) mission seeks) .

- Require the P3 model, i.e. “Pro Planet People”: large-scale change starts with personal choices

- Respecting indigenous knowledge and the rights of local communities is vital, as they often possess sustainable practices that have preserved ecosystems for generations.

- The business sector must rethink its relationship with the environment, shifting from short-term profit maximization to long-term ecological balance.

- Promoting “All Life Approach” (Like the Philosophy of Jainism) , can reshape humanity’s place within the natural world and encourages a deeper respect for the environment, pushing society toward practices that protect all forms of life rather than exploiting them.

- This transformation can be achieved through education, where environmental ethics are taught fromanearlyage,fosteringasenseofresponsibility and accountability in future generations. (Finland Model -incorporate sustainability into their national curriculum)

- Additionally, promoting sustainable consumption habits (requires initiative such as “Earth Hour”) , reducing reliance on fossil fuels can significantly lower environmental impacts.

- Technologies that aid in climate mitigation—such as renewable energy, nature-based solutions, and urban rewilding—must be deployed in ways that respect ecological limits.

- Global Cooperation and Solidarity: Climate change is a global issue requiring international cooperation and solidarity. Projects like Africa’s Great Green Wall, which seeks to halt desertification, illustrate how nations can collaborate to address environmental challenges. Sharing knowledge, resources, and technology across borders will enable more effective climate action and foster a spirit of global responsibility. **Conclusion** Ending the destructive cycle of human greed and overconsumption to protect life on Earth requires a radical transformation of values, policies, and actions. Like the cautionary tale of Bhasmasura, unchecked powerandgreedcanleadtocatastrophicconsequences, but by choosing a path of sustainability, we can restore balance and build a future that is equitable, just, and respectfulofourplanet’sfiniteresources.Onlythrough collective effort can we bring equilibrium between society and the environment, safeguarding life for generations to come.

## Question 5(a) (Year: 2024 | Paper: GS IV) **(a) “Learn everything that is good from others, but bring it in, and in your own way absorb it, do not become others.” - Swami Vivekananda (150 Words, 10 Marks)** ### Answer **Answer:** **Introduction:** The above mentioned quotation gives an impression that one must learn, absorb, understand and bring in good things from surroundings but not in a verbatim manner rather by making adjustments so as to favour one’s own condition.
**Question ID: 2024-gs4-q5-unacademy**

- In an era of globalization, where Western cultural hegemony often influences local traditions, this quoteremindsusoftheimportanceofmaintaining our cultural identity. It advocates for a balance between global integration and local authenticity, often referred to as **“Glocalization.”** - The quote cautions against losing one’s individuality in the process of learning. Eg: During **Perestroika,** the USSR attempted to shift to a capitalist model by adopting Western practices. However, this transition was often superficial and lacked a deep understanding, USSR failed to make adaptations as per its needs and this led to its eventual disintegration.

- Blind imitation, especially without contextual understanding, may lead to loss of one’s cultural identity or ethical missteps. Eg: **Raja Ram Mohon** **Roy** advocated for Western education to foster social reform in India while rejecting Western hegemony.

- In the public sphere, leaders often face pressure to conformtopopularopinionsorinternationaltrends. However, ethical leadership requires discernment, ensuring that borrowed ideas align with one’s own ethical framework and responsibilities. Eg: The **Non-Alignment Movement (NAM)** embodied the idea of absorbing the best from both the U.S. and Soviet blocs while maintaining independence. By strategically leveraging elements from both sides, NAM prevented many Third World countries from being drawn into power blocs, allowing them to develop their own paths and identities. Mahatma Gandhi famously said **“I do not want my** **house to be walled in on all sides and my windows to** **be stuffed. I want the culture of all lands to be blown** **about my house as freely as possible. But I refuse to be** **blown off my feet by any.”** In the future, the ability to learn from diverse global influences while preserving cultural identity will be vital.Byadaptingsuccessfulpracticestolocalcontexts, nations can drive innovation and social progress. The quote advocates for learning with discernment, fostering ethical integrity, and ensuring that borrowed knowledge aligns with the individual’s or society’s core values. **(b)“Faithisofnoavailintheabsenceofstrength.Faith** **andstrength,bothareessentialtoaccomplishany** **great work.” - Sardar Patel (150 Words, 10 Marks)** 
**Answer:** **Introduction:** This quote means that simply believing in something (faith) isn’t enough to achieve significant goals. One also needs the strength—whether it’s physical, mental, or emotional—to turn that belief into action.

- Faith alone, without the necessary strength to pursue it, can lead to complacency or wishful thinking. Eg: **Sardar Patel** envisioned a united India after independence, but his faith alone was not enough. He demonstrated the strength needed to achieve this vision by skillfully using diplomacy and,whennecessary,forcetointegratetheprincely states.

- Similarly, **ISRO’s journey to the Moon** perfectly illustrates the idea that belief must be paired with capability to achieve great goals. Their faith in the Chandrayaan missions inspired ambitious plans, but it was the organization’s technical expertise and skilled scientists that brought this vision to life.

- **Lord Rama** had an unwavering faith in his mission to rescue Goddess Sita and restore dharma. However, it was his strength—both in terms of physical prowess and strategic alliances, such as with Lord Hanuman and the monkey army—that enabled him to achieve this great task.

- Strength without faith or purpose becomes directionless. Physical or intellectual strength may lead to short-term gains, but without a guiding principle or belief, it may result in outcomes that are either morally ambiguous or unsustainable. Faith gives strength a sense of direction, aligning it with ethical, long-term objectives. Eg: While human strength has manifested in the creation of powerful technologies like **artificial intelligence,** the faith in their ethical and humane use must guide them. Otherwise, they could lead to harmful societal consequences, such as **job displacement** or **increased inequality.** Mahatma Gandhi said **“Strength does not come from** **physical capacity. It comes from an indomitable will.”** Combining faith and strength empowers individuals andcommunitiestotransformtheirvisionsintoreality. When belief in a goal is matched with the necessary capabilities, obstacles can be overcome, leading to meaningful change.

## Question 6(c) (Year: 2024 | Paper: GS IV) **(c) “Inlaw,amanisguiltywhenheviolatestherights of others. In ethics, he is guilty if he only thinks of doing so.” - Immanuel Kant (150 Words, 10 Marks)** ### Answer **Answer:** **Introduction:** The above quote states that in the field of law a man is judged based on his actions while in the field of ethics he is responsible for even his thinking. While law does not take into account the act of omission or intention, Ethics acts as a yardstick that captures a broader perspective by taking note of Intentions, thoughtprocess and acts of Omission.
**Question ID: 2024-gs4-q6-unacademy**

- Morality is not solely judged by actions but also by the intentions and inner deliberations that guide behavior. Eg: Someone who is considering **cheating** in a test may have all the knowledge required to succeed honestly but is tempted by laziness or personal gain. Even though he hasn’t done anything wrong yet, his thoughts alone would make him morally guilty.

- Ethical purity extends beyond mere external conduct to the very thoughts we harbor. Eg: **Mahatma Gandhi’s** concept of **Satyagraha** emphasizes that both intentions and actions are crucial in the pursuit of justice. He believed that even contemplating harm against others makes one morally guilty, as true non-violence requires compassion at all levels.

- Ethical living demands more than just compliance with laws; it requires us to cultivate virtue, selfdiscipline, and a strong moral sense. Eg: A **civil** **servant** may have the discretion to allocate resources for development activities. However, if he grants more funding to a project in which he has a personal stake, it may not be legally wrong but would render him ethically guilty. Law punishes actions that violate rights, while ethics concerns harmful intentions. Ethical leadership and behavior demand accountability for thoughts, not just actions, fostering a more conscientious and morally robust society. Together, they promote a society rooted in respect, responsibility, and moral integrity.

## Question 7(a) (Year: 2024 | Paper: GS IV) **Q4.(a) “The concept of Just and Unjust is contextual. What was just a year back, may turn out to be unjust in today’s context. Changing context should be constantly under scrutiny to prevent miscarriage of justice.” Examine the above statement with suitable examples. (150 Words, 10 Marks)** ### Answer **Answer:** **Introduction:** The concept of justice is not static. Justice depend the moralandethicalstandardsofasocietyatagiventime. It evolves with the changing societal norms, cultural values, and technological advancements.
**Question ID: 2024-gs4-q7-unacademy**

## **Body** ## **Examples** - **LGBTQ+ Rights:** A few decades ago, same-sex relationshipswerecriminalizedinmanycountries. What was considered “just” thens now widely recognized as unjust discrimination.

- **Child Labor:** During the Industrial Revolution, child labor was common and often seen as economically necessary. Today, it’s widely recognized as exploitative and unjust.

- To prevent miscarriages of justice, it is essential to continually **scrutinize the changing contexts.** - **Education and open discourse** are to foster understanding and reevaluation of outdated or unjust practices.

 - Legislatures must also be proactive in **amending laws** to reflect new understandings of justice.

- **The judiciary** plays a pivotal role in interpreting laws in light of contemporary contexts. Judicial activismcan help bridge thegap between **outdated** **legislation and current societal values.** - Section 377 of the Indian Penal Code, which criminalized homosexual acts. With change in societal attitudes it was eventually decriminalized in 2018. **Conclusion:** Justice is a fluid concept, continually redefined by the evolving contexts of society. Recognizing the contextual nature of justice is crucial in ensuring that laws and practices remain fair and equitable. **4.(b)“Mindless addiction to Form, ignoring the** **Substance of the matter, results in rendering of** **injustice. A perceptive civil servant is one who** **ignores such literalness and carries out true** **intent,” Examine the above statement with suita-** **ble illustrations. (150 Words, 10 Marks)** 
**Answer:** **Introduction:** This statement highlights the importance of understanding and implementing the spirit of the law or policy rather than blindly following its letter.

## **Body** ## **Mindless addiction to form ignoring substance** - Thisreferstothepracticeofrigidlyfollowingrules, regulations, or procedures without considering their underlying purpose.

- When people or institutions become overly focusedontechnicalitiesandformalities,theymay **inadvertently cause harm or injustice.** - **Examples:** A citizen applies for Aadhar card, but a civil servant rejects the application because the provided utility bill as proof of residence **is 32 days** **old.** The rule states that the proof should not be older than **30 days.** **A perceptive civil servant ignoring literalness and** ## **carrying out true intent** - This part of the statement emphasizes the importance of understanding the **underlying** **purpose of laws, rules, or policies.** - A perceptive civil servant is one who can look beyond the literal interpretation and make decisions that align with the true intent or spirit of the regulation.

- **Examples:** In environmental regulations, a factory may meet emission targets by outsourcing its polluting processes, reducing emissions on paper. However,thisviolatesthelaw’sintenttocutoverall emissions, not just relocate them.

- This approach, however, requires **a delicate** **balance.** While it’s important to consider the spirit of the law, completely disregarding established rules and procedures could lead to **arbitrariness** **and potential abuse of power.** **Conclusion:** The given statement advocates for a **more nuanced** **and thoughtful approach** to governance and rule application, emphasizing the importance of substance over form . **5.(a)”The ‘Code of Conduct’ and ‘Code of Ethics’ are** **the sources of guidance in public administration.** **There is code of conduct already in operation,** <p align="center"><img src="images/unacademy_p81_img99.png" alt="Diagram" /></p> **whereas code of ethics is not yet put in place.** **Suggest a suitable model for code of ethics to** **maintain integrity, probity and transparency in** **governance. (150 Words, 10 Marks)** 
**Answer:** **Introduction:** **Code of Conduct:** These are sets of specific rules that guide the actions of public officials. These are already in operation in many public administration systems. It aims to prevent unethical practices like conflicts of interest, bribery, and self-dealing by clearly defining acceptable and unacceptable actions. **Code of Ethics:** It acts like a set of values, similar to a constitution, that guide how decisions are made and how people should behave. They focus on key values like honesty, fairness, accountability, and serving the public. In tricky situations, the Code of Ethics helps people choose what’s right based on these values. **Suitablemodelforcodeofethicstomaintainintegrity,** ## **probity and transparency in governance** - **Core Ethical Principles:** Core ethical principles guide ethical conduct by promoting integrity, where individuals uphold strong moral values, and accountability, taking responsibility for their actions. Transparency ensures openness, while impartiality calls for unbiased, merit-based decisions.

- **Conflict of Interest Guidelines:** Provide clear guidance on identifying and managing potential conflicts of interest. Require regular disclosure of personal and financial interests. Establish procedures for recusal when conflicts arise.

- **Anti-Corruption Measures:** Strict rules against accepting gifts, bribes, or other inducements should be clearly articulated.

- **Whistleblower Protection:** Create a safe environment for reporting ethical violations. Establishing clear procedures for investigating reported violations and protecting whistleblowers from retaliation.

- **Enforcement Mechanism:** Proper enforcement mechanisms like ethics committee should be there and the code should be periodically reviewed.

- **Integration** **with** **Existing** **Frameworks:** Integrating with existing laws, regulations, and the Code of Conduct and Guiding resolving conflicts between different ethical obligations for effective implementation of the Code.

## **Indian Examples** - **Code of Ethics for Civil Servants in India:** The Second Administrative Reforms Commission (ARC) emphasizes **integrity, impartiality, and** **publicserviceaskeyethicalstandards.Itpromotes** adherencetotheConstitution,safeguardingpublic interest, and ethical governance in decisionmaking.

- **Public Service Values and Ethics in Indian Civil** **Services:** Grounded in Indian ethos, this code promotes **constitutional values, public welfare,** **and national development.** Ethical principles like **seva (service)** and **satya (truthfulness)** guide civil servants toward societal benefits.

- **L. K. Jha Committee Report (1966):** The report highlighted the need for **impartiality, objectivity,** **and transparency** in public administration to combat corruption. It recommended integrating ethics into decision-making for enhanced public trust.

- **Citizen’s Charter in India:** This initiative aims to enhance **accountability and transparency** by defining service standards for public agencies. It reflects a commitment to **good governance** and responsive service delivery to citizens.

## **Conclusion** - A well-designed Code of Ethics is crucial for maintaining integrity, probity, and transparency in governance.

- The model proposed combines ethical principles with practical enforcement mechanisms, ensuring that public servants are held accountable while fostering a culture of trust and transparency.

- By implementing these strategies, the government can promote a more ethical, transparent, and efficient public administration. **5(b)The soul of the new law, Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita** **(BNS) is Justice, Equality and Impartiality based** **on Indian culture and ethos. Discuss this in the** **lightofmajorshiftfromadoctrineofpunishment** **to justice in the present judicial system. (150** **Words, 10 Marks)** 
**Answer:** **Introduction:** **Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita (BNS)** The Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita (BNS) is India’s new criminal code, which came into effect on July 1, 2024. It replaced the Indian Penal Code (IPC), which was in place since British India. BNS seeks to create a more human-centric judicial system, focusing on fairness, rehabilitation, and restorative justice, rather than merely punitive measures.

## **Indian Culture and Ethos in Judicial Philosophy** - Indianculturetraditionallyvaluesconceptssuchas **dharma (duty and righteousness), karma (action** **and consequence),** and **ahimsa (non-violence).** - These principles are echoed in the BNS, which seekstocraftalegalsystemthatisnotonlypunitive but also compassionate and aimed at the moral upliftment of society.

- The idea is that justice should be holistic, addressing not just the individual’s wrongdoing but also the larger societal impact, while allowing for the possibility of forgiveness and reform. **Major Shifts Under Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita (BNS)** ## **y Justice Over Punishment** - The BNS shifts the focus from punishment to **justice** not just for the state but for the individual and society.

## **y Equality and Fairness** - The new law seeks to uphold **equality** in justice, recognizing that justice should not be influenced by wealth, status, caste, or other biases.

## **y Impartiality Reflecting Indian Ethos** - Unlike the earlier framework, the BNS is informed by **Indian cultural ethos** and values.

 - Traditional Indian jurisprudence has often emphasized **restorative justice** and **community reconciliation.** ## **y Restorative Justice** - A fundamental feature of the BNS is its **restorative justice** approach, where the focus is on **repairing harm** rather than exacting revenge.

 - Indian culture has long emphasized the importance of **restoring balance** within society and relationships.

## **y Focus on Human Rights** - The BNS is aligned with modern principles of **human rights,** ensuring that laws are interpreted and applied in ways that respect the dignity of individuals.

 - The emphasis on **justice and equality** reflects India’s constitutional commitment to uphold fundamental rights and ensure fairness in the legal process, which contrasts with the often rigid, punitive nature of colonial laws.

## **y Simplification of Laws** - TheBNSseekstosimplifyandmodernizelaws to make them more accessible to common citizens.

 - Bydoingso,theaimistopromotetransparency and **understanding** of the law, ensuring that justice is not denied due to procedural complexities or outdated legal provisions.

## **y Alternative dispute resolution** - The BNS encourages alternative dispute mechanismslikemediationandreconciliation, shifting away from the rigid doctrine of punishment toward restorative justice. **ExampleofShiftinLawfromadoctrineofpunishment** **to justice** One example of this shift is seen in the approach toward **minor offenses, non-violent crimes, and first-** **time offenders,** where reformative measures such as **community service** or **counseling** might be preferred over imprisonment. Similarly, there is a growing recognition of the **restorative potential** in cases of personal disputes or civil wrongs, where mediation and reconciliation are encouraged to foster social harmony.

## **Conclusion** - The Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita represents a pivotal moment in India’s legal evolution, prioritizing justice, equality, and impartiality.

- This transformation from doctrine of punishment to justice represents India’s commitment to crafting a judicial philosophy that honors its cultural heritage while meeting modern standards of human rights and justice.

## Question 8(a) (Year: 2024 | Paper: GS IV) **Q6.(a)”In Indian culture and value system, an equal opportunity has been provided irrespective of gender identity. The number of women in public service has been steadily increasing over the years” . Examine the gender-specific challenges faced by female public servants and suggest suitable measures to increase their efficiency in discharging their duties and maintaining high standards of probity. (150 Words, 10 Marks)** ### Answer **Answer:** **Introduction:** India’s constitutional framework emphasizes equality, particularly under **Article 16** .It guarantees equality of opportunity in matters of public employment, prohibiting discrimination based on gender. This framework has paved the way for women’s increasing participation in public service. However, despite these constitutional safeguards and legal provisions, female public servants continue to face gender-specific challenges that hinder their full potential. **Gender-Specific Challenges Faced by Female Public** ## **Servants** ## **y Workplace Discrimination and Bias** - Women in public service often face gender discrimination, which limits their access to leadership roles.
**Question ID: 2024-gs4-q8-unacademy**

 - Forinstance,Stereotypingwomenasincapable of handling “tough” assignments or field postings.

## **y Lack of Gender-Sensitive Infrastructure** - Inadequate provisions for safety, sanitation, and gender-specific needs in offices and field postings can hinder women’s productivity.

**Question ID: 2023-gs4-q11-unacademy**

 - **Example:** Aid given by IMF/UN to Pakistan for humanitarian needs often diverted for non-humanitarian uses.

 - **Example:** Operation Dost by India to aid Turkey and Syria during the 2023 earthquake.

- **Respect for Sovereignty** - Conditional aid often undermines sovereignty of recipient nations.

 - **Example:** IMF loan conditions during India’s 1991 crisis.

- **Avoidance of Debt-Trap Diplomacy** - Loans should not be instruments of dominance.

 - **Example:** China’s Belt and Road loans to Pakistan, leading to debt dependency.

- **Local Capacity Building** - Aidshouldempowercommunitiesratherthan create dependency.

 - **Example:** India’s support to Bhutan in education, healthcare, and infrastructure.

- **Intent of Aid** - Should be humanitarian and developmental, not strategic or political.

 - **Example:** U.S. aid to Pakistan during the Afghan war was driven by strategic interests more than humanitarian concerns. **Conclusion** While international aid is necessary, its ethical delivery is equally important. ## Question 20(a) (Year: 2023 | Paper: GS IV) **Q6(a)What were the major teachings of Guru Nanak? Explain their relevance in the contemporary world. (10 marks, 150 words)** ### Answer **Answer:** **Introduction** Guru Nanak (1469–1539), the founder of Sikhism, was a spiritual reformer who challenged ritualism, caste divisions, and social injustice. His teachings emphasizeddevotiontooneGod,equality,compassion, and honest living. Rooted in universal human values, his philosophy continues to inspire ethical conduct and social harmony across cultures. **Major Teachings of Guru Nanak** 1. **Belief in One God** - Stressed the existence of a single, formless, and omnipresent God.
**Question ID: 2023-gs4-q20-unacademy**

 - Rejected idol worship and rituals.

2. **Equality and Non-Discrimination** - Condemned caste system and upheld equality of all human beings, irrespective of religion, caste, or gender.

3. **Direct Access to God** - Advocated a personal connection with God without the need for intermediaries or rituals.

4. **Honest Living and Hard Work** - Encouraged earning through ethical means (Kirat Karo), without resorting to fraud or exploitation.

5. **Rejecting Asceticism** - Opposed withdrawal from society for spiritual enlightenment. Emphasized active participation in community life.

6. **Contentment and Detachment** - Promoted inner peace, self-restraint, and freedom from materialistic greed. **Relevance in the Contemporary World** - **Promotes Religious Harmony:** Belief in one God fosters tolerance and interfaith understanding in today’s plural societies.

- **Addresses Social Inequality:** Teachings of equality challenge caste and gender discrimination still prevalent in many societies.

- **Spiritual Autonomy:** Direct access to God resonates with modern emphasis on individual freedom and spirituality.

- **Ethical Conduct in Public Life:** Honest work and integrity remain crucial for sustainable economic and social progress.

- **Balance Between Material and Spiritual:** His rejection of asceticism is relevant in today’s consumer-driven culture, emphasizing balance and moderation.

- **Sustainable Living:** Contentment and detachment counter overconsumption and environmental exploitation. **Conclusion** Guru Nanak’s teachings are timeless, offering solutions to contemporary issues of inequality, intolerance, materialism, and social injustice. By emphasizing equality,honesty,andcompassion,hismessageremains a guiding light for building a just and harmonious world.

For instance, if an officer is preoccupied with administrative formalities and protocol, which constitute the periphery, they may fail to address pressing issues like poverty alleviation, infrastructure development, and quality education provision, which form the core of their responsibilities.

- A **bureaucracy embroiled in the inconsequential** **may lose sight of its fundamental purpose of** **serving the public.** In this regard, inefficiency in service delivery and impaired governance become the unfortunate byproducts of such misplaced priorities.

- However, it’s important to note that **peripheral** **issues are not irrelevant.** They often **deal with** **the procedures and protocols designed to ensure** **consistency, transparency, and accountability** in the system.

- The **key lies in striking a balance between core** **and peripheral issues,** ensuring that the latter doesn’t overshadow the former. In addressing this concern, measures such as regular audits, robust feedback mechanisms, and capacity building can be instrumental.

## Question 31 (Year: 2022 | Paper: GS IV) **Q5. (a) Russia and Ukraine war has been going on for the last seven months. Different countries have taken independent stands and actions keeping in view their own national interests. We are all aware that war has its own impact on the different aspects of society, including human tragedy. What are those ethical issues that are crucial to be considered while launching the war and its continuation so far? Illustrate with justification the ethical issues involved in the given state of affairs. Approach: y Introduction: Acknowledge the complexity of ethical dilemmas in the context of war. y Body: Discuss various ethical issues that arise in the context of a war such as respect for sovereignty, just cause, proportionality, noncombatant immunity, responsibility of the international community, propaganda and misinformation, and post-war justice and reconciliation. y Conclusion: Emphasize the need for ethical considerations in the actions of the warring parties and the international community.** ### Answer **Answer:** War always presents complex ethical dilemmas, particularly in the context of global politics where national interests and international norms often conflict. There are some ethical issues that are crucial to consider in the context of launching a war and its continuation.
**Question ID: 2022-gs4-q31-unacademy**

- **Respect for Sovereignty:** Every country has the righttoself-determinationandterritorialintegrity. If Russia is found to have violated Ukraine’s sovereignty without a justifiable cause, it would raise ethical questions about international law and respect for national boundaries.

- **Just Cause:** A central question is whether the war is fought for a just cause. According to just war theory, a war is only ethically justifiable to prevent serious harm, such as aggression or genocide. If the war was initiated for strategic, economic, or territorial gains, it would be seen as unethical.

- **Proportionality:** Even in a justified war, the harm caused by warfare must be proportional to the benefits expected. The suffering inflicted on civilians, destruction of infrastructure, and economic harm must be weighed against the objectives of the war. If disproportionate harm is inflicted, it can be ethically indefensible.

- **Discrimination or Non-combatant Immunity:** International humanitarian law demands a clear distinction between combatants and noncombatants. Indiscriminate attacks leading to civilian casualties pose severe ethical issues. If non-combatants are being targeted, it clearly violates ethical norms and conventions of warfare.

- **Responsibility of International Community:** The international community has a responsibility to protect human rights, prevent genocide, and uphold international peace. Their response to the conflict, be it diplomatic pressure, sanctions, or military intervention, also raises ethical issues. If they fail to act or if their actions exacerbate the situation, it becomes an ethical concern.

- **Propaganda and Misinformation:** In today’s information age, warfare is not only physical but also psychological. The spread of propaganda and misinformation to manipulate public opinion is a significant ethical issue. It not only misleads citizens but also hinders peaceful resolution efforts.

- **Post-war Justice and Reconciliation:** Ethical considerations also extend to the post-war phase. Ensuring justice for war crimes, providing humanitarian aid, rebuilding efforts, and fostering reconciliation are crucial for long-term peace. In the context of the Russia-Ukraine war, these ethical considerations should guide the actions of the warring parties and the international community. Each stakeholder should act in a way that respects human rights, minimizes harm, upholds international law, and seeks a peaceful resolution to the conflict.

However, their actions often place them at significant risk of retaliation and victimization. <p align="center"><img src="images/unacademy_p246_img89.png" alt="Diagram" /></p>** Policy Measures To Be Taken To Safeguard **##** Whistleblowers **-** Legal Protection: **Implement comprehensive legislation that explicitly protects whistleblowers from retaliation, including dismissal, demotion, or harassment. -** Compensation and Support: **Ensure that whistleblowerswhosufferretaliationhaveaccessto remedies, including reinstatement, compensation for lost wages and other damages, and support services such as legal advice and counseling.

-** Awareness and Training: **Raise awareness about the rights and protections for whistleblowers, and train managers and staff on how to handle whistleblower reports.

-** Protections Beyond Employment: **Broaden protections to cover not only workplace retaliation but also other forms of detriment, such as defamation lawsuits or criminal prosecution.

-** External Reporting: **Allow for disclosures to be made not only within the organization but also to external entities, such as regulatory bodies, law enforcement agencies, and the media, especially when internal channels are ineffective or unresponsive.

-** Culture of Integrity: **Foster a culture of integrity andopennessintheorganizationwhereemployees feel safe to speak up about wrongdoing without fear of reprisal.

-** Regular Review: **Regularly review and update whistleblower policies and protections to ensure their effectiveness. The following points critically examine whether CSR is efficient and sufficient enough to fulfill the social roles and responsibilities in the corporate sector.

##** y Positive Impacts of CSR **-** Community Development: **CSR programs ofteninvolveinvestmentinlocalcommunities, which can foster socioeconomic growth and development.

 -** Environmental Stewardship: **Many CSR initiativesfocusonreducingtheenvironmental impact of business operations and promoting sustainable practices.

 -** Reputation Enhancement: **A strong CSR program can improve a company’s public image, leading to increased customer loyalty and potentially higher profits.

##** y Limitations and Criticisms of CSR **-** Surface-Level Commitments: **Critics argue that some companies use CSR primarily as a public relations tool rather than making substantivechangestotheirbusinesspractices.

 -** Lack of Accountability: **Because CSR is often voluntary, companies may not be held accountable for failing to meet their stated CSR goals.

 -** DiversionfromCoreIssues:Thereisaconcern **that CSR may be used to distract from harmful business practices or to provide a veneer of responsibility without addressing underlying issues.

 -** Need for Systemic Change: **Addressing these challenges requires a comprehensive approach involvingpolicychanges,regulation,andshifts in societal attitudes towards sustainability and social justice.

 -** Transparent Reporting: **Companies should be encouraged or required to report on their CSR initiatives and their impacts in a transparent and standardized manner.

## Question 1(a) (Year: 2021 | Paper: GS IV)** Q1 (a). Identify five ethical traits on which one can plot the performance of a civil servant. Justify their inclusion in the matrix. (10 Marks, 150 Words) Tags: Aptitude and foundational values for Civil Service, integrity, impartiality and non- partisanship, objectivity, dedication to public service, empathy, tolerance and compassion towards the weaker sections. **### Answer

##** Decoding the Question **-** Introduction: Define ethics. **-** Body: Give a matrix of ethical traits for** **performance evaluation of civil servants.** - **Conclusion: Conclude suitably.** **Answer:** A personal standard of what is right and wrong is called ethics. Since ethics in the civil service is ongoing research that cannot be quantitatively justified by a few conditions, its importance is significant. As representatives of the public, civil workers have a responsibility to observe the highest ethical standards. The highest priority for a civil servant is to conduct themselves ethically. When assessing a civil servant’s performance,amatrixofethicalqualitiescanbeuseful. **Matrix of Ethical Traits Which Civil Servants Must** ## **Hold at all the Times** - **Emotional intelligence:** This demands the neutrality, impartiality, superiority, and logic of an administrator since it fosters a democratic workplace culture and self-awareness. Outcome and effectiveness orientation is a component of emotional intelligence.

- **Integrity:** Integrity is necessary to avoid corruption, utilise resources wisely, pursue professional excellence, and serve as an example for others.

- **Fortitude:** The bravery to speak out for the truth and against any potential injustice that is tolerated in society, which appears to be an impediment to everything that is right, is what is meant by having fortitude.

- **Compassion:** This signifies the empathy for the disadvantaged group and the drive to fight for the weak.

- **Objectivity:** This enables a government servant to make impartial and nonpartisan choices without personal bias; to act fairly; and tolerating alternative opinions. Since ethics is a constant investigation that cannot be quantitatively justified by a few factors, it is essential in the civil service. Although decision-making processes are the foundation of governance, the most crucial aspect is ongoing improvement in decisionmaking quality. These five moral qualities serve as a public servant’s moral foundation, and from them, other values such as non-partisanship, tolerance, and responsiveness may emerge. These qualities make sure a government worker does his duties even under challenging circumstances.

They thus offer a foundation for the accomplishment of organisational goals that cannot be attained by just steering toward those goals alone and are deserving of further analysis in the context of the growth of public services. There are many different values, but the **Nolan Committee** **and the 2nd ARC Report** have highlighted those that support public employees, or those who work for and in government, in performing their jobs effectively. **Essential Values that are Required to be an Effective** ## **Public Servant** - **Justice** is the capacity to make decisions without taking one’s own feelings or interests into account, as well as the associated fairness. These ideas of justice and fairness are necessary in the civil service to make sure that the common people receive their just compensation without prejudice, inefficiency, or greed.

- Elements of openness include transparency and inspection-openness.

- The capacity to respond to the needs of the general public is **responsiveness.** - **Empathy:** The capacity for empathy is the capacity to experience and act in a way that lessens the pain of others.

- **Selflessness:** Actions should be directed at helping others rather than oneself.

- Honestyencompassestraitslikefairness,reliability, and sincerity.

- Because public officials are ultimately driven to serve the interests of the people, **tolerance and** **humanity** are crucial. **Strategies to Prevent Unethical Behaviour Among** ## **Public Employees** - Effective laws, rules, and regulations that clearly define dos and don’ts and have a procedure for prompt retaliation for unethical behaviour.

- Limiting the use of discretionary authority and avoiding direct interactions between service users and providers in order to minimise opportunities for rent seeking.

- Effective use of RT, social audits, e-governance, and other **transparency and accountability tools** is required. Giving **whistle-blowers** the right protection is also essential.

- **PersonnelManagement:Itmakesiteasiertochoose** people with high ethical aptitude and a stirred conscience, as well as to give them performancebased promotions and regular training. The public, who depend on the public service, may stoprespectingandtrustingitifitsworkisnotfounded on or guided by the right set of principles. Depending on the administrative and political interests of the moment, different values may receive varied emphasis; yet stability and coherence require adherence to a set of broadly consistent and recognised ideals. The combination of these fundamental principles and these measures can increase the effectiveness and civic engagement of government employees.

Each step brings expectation and obstacles along. These obstacles should not frighten oneself as these are normal parts of life and also enable growth through experiences. It also stimulates resilience capacities. **Perseverance is a** **persistent attempt to do or accomplish something.** It is also referred to as a continued effort to do or achieve something despite difficulties, failure and opposition. Example: During the freedom struggle, Gandhi and other freedom fighters demonstrated perseverance in theirpursuitofindependence.Anotherexamplewhere it’s needed the most in current times is that during emergency circumstances such as mob violence, natural disasters (cyclones), and so on, civil servants must persevere in order to maintain law and order and restore peace. **There have been numerous examples of great** **personalities in history who faced challenges but** ## **succeeded, such as** - **Thomas Edison** tried 2000 different materials in searchoffilamentforthelightbulbbutfailed.When his assistant complained, “All our efforts were in vain “. Edison replied, “We have come a long way as now we know we cannot use 2000 elements to make a good bulb.” It was his perseverance which earned him the merit of being one of the most prolific inventors.

- **Mahatma Gandhi** pioneered the Indian freedom struggle. He faced many challenges that the British empire created. Even then, it was not an easy task for him, and the Satyagraha Movement was not without its challenges. He kept persevering and finallyplayedamajorpartingainingindependence for India.

- The creator of Reliance, **Dhirubhai Ambani,** did not come from a wealthy family and had a humble upbringing. At the age of 16, he relocated to Yemen where he worked as a clerk. But he realised he had to pursue his calling, so he risked it all and came back to India to start his company with a close associate. Even though Ambani and Champaklal Damani had opposing viewpoints and decided to part ways, Ambani persisted in his business and even made the decision to trade stocks. His achievements in the stock market and business transactions have been criticised frequently, but he attained power via perseverance and determination.

- **Mary Kom** is a five-time successive World Boxing champion who has overcome gender bias, poverty, the limitations of her small size, and the disadvantages of the small region to win the Olympic medal for boxing through her perseverance and determination. She is an inspiration not only for the North Eastern people but also for women who endure hardships on a daily basis. Perseverance is a key characteristic of a person with a positive attitude which is developed as a result of socialisation by different sources like family, peer group, leadership, self-belief. It is one’s attitude that enables him to keep walking despite falling multiple times. **According to Mark Cuban “Winners make** **most errors as they never quit”.** Mount Everest was not climbed in one day, the space mission was not successful at its first attempt, social changes took eras to be adopted. Thus, perseverance is a tool which enables us to see the ray at the end of the tunnel. **(b). “We can never obtain peace in the outer world** **untilandunlessweobtainpeacewithinourselves.”—** **Dalai Lama. (10 Marks, 150 Words)** **Tags: Aptitude and foundational values for** **Civil Service, integrity, impartiality and non-** **partisanship, objectivity, dedication to public** **service, empathy, tolerance and compassion** **towards the weaker-sections.** ## **Decoding the Question** - **Introduction: Start with explaining the** **meaning of the given quote.** ## **y Body** - **Define and mention the advantages of** **inner peace.** - **Describe how inner peace can achieve** **outer peace and how one can develop** **inner peace.** - **Conclusion: Conclude with stating a famous** **quote.** **Answer:** The Dalai Lama was awarded the Nobel Peace Prize (1989) for advocating a peaceful solution based upon tolerance, mutual respect in order to preserve the historical and cultural heritage of his people. He **defines peace not merely as absence of war but** **where human rights and nations are free.** While defining peace, he talked about outer peace which can be achieved by peace within ourselves i.e., inner peace.

## **Advantages of Inner Peace** - Can easily trust or believe in yourself.

- One can stay calm in all situations.

- Reduces mental, physical illness.

- It creates a path towards happiness.

- Helps to avoid negative thoughts.

- Helps to stay focused on your goals. After the devastating war of Kalinga, Ashoka renounced war and took the policy of Dhamma. Only when he achieved his inner peace, he could establish the outer peace. Hence **through inner peace one can** ## **achieve outer peace that calls for** - **Societal harmony:** Absence of hostility among social groups.

- **Equality:** Respect, equality between all identity groups.

- **Fraternity, brotherhood:** Between different religions, groups and nations.

- **Sustainable future:** Where everyone has equal rights and equal access to resources and lives a dignified life.

- **Religious harmony:** All religions agree upon the necessity to control the undisciplined mind that harbours selfishness and each teaches a path leading to a spiritual state (i.e peaceful, ethical, disciplined, etc.)

## **How one can develop inner peace** - **Compassion:** Compassionate people have a balance of mind and physical health which leads to positive emotion in life.

- **Tolerance:** It is a threshold of achieving peace and developing a mind for absorbing multiculturalism. It is essential for the realisation of Human Right (UNESCO).

- **Emotional Intelligence (EI):** Through EI, one can control negative emotions.

- **Strategy to ensure Inner peace:** Remembering one’s own worth, avoiding doing things with illwill, maintaining distance with toxic people, and regular meditation can bring inner peace. Inner peace is a state of calm, serenity that arises due to having no sufferings such as greed, desire, hatred, ill will, etc. According to J L Nehru, **lasting peace can** **come to a peaceful mind and through compassion,** **tolerance and emotional intelligence one can achieve** **a peaceful mind.** ---

## Question 3(c) (Year: 2021 | Paper: GS IV) **(c) “Life doesn’t make any sense without interdependence. We need each other, and the sooner we learn that, it is better for us all.” -Erik Erikson (10 Marks, 150 Words) Tags: Human Values** ### Answer

## **Decoding the Question** - **Introduction: Explain the given quote.** ## **y Body** - **Mention** **the** **importance** **of** **interdependence.** - **Write about the different manifestations** **of interdependence.** - **Conclusion: Conclude with the significance** **of interdependence.** **Answer:** **“Lifedoesn’tmakeanysensewithoutinterdependence.** **We need each other, and the sooner we learn that, it** **is better for us all.”** This is the quote of Erik Erikson, who was a German-American developmental psychologist and psychoanalyst, known for his theory on psychological development of human beings. This quote underlines an essential virtue of living beings on the earth which follows the principle of interdependence. Humans are social beings; they cannot thrive in isolation and the kind of life one will lead depends upon how soon oce accepts this fact. The importance of interdependence was realised during the COVID-19 lockdown phase, where people came together voluntarily to help the needy with oxygen cylinders, food grains and other essentials.

## **Different Manifestation of Interdependence** - **Individual level:** Right from birth till old age, people play different roles of interdependence as son/daughter, adult, husband/ wife, etc. Individuals are dependent on various agencies such as family, school, peer group, media, etc., for their socialisation and the values of individuals shape the moral fabric of the society.

- **Societal level:** The society works as an organic structure where interdependence plays a vital role. For example: in the crisis of COVID society contributed immensely to tackle emergency protocols placed due to pandemic. There are many forms of social interdependence in terms of economy, region, etc.

- **National level:** The interplay between central and state government is another example of interdependence. Be it policy making, executive action, conflict resolution, serving poor interdependence is the core underlying feature. To sustain democracy; the system of checks and balances,distributionofpowerbetweenlegislative, judiciary, executive is necessary. Similarly, development of social capital is important for a nation’s progress.

- **Global level:** The growing interdependence of world’s economies, culture, population, brought about by cross border trade in goods and services, technology, flow of investment, people and information. For example: **India has upheld** **the value of ‘vasudhaiva kutumbakam’** in its engagement with the world. India’s upper hand in generic medicines has been helpful in tackling diseases like HIV AIDS in poorer countries of Africa, and similarly with respect to COVID vaccinesaswell.Withregardstoelectronicgadgets a picture of interdependence can be observed. For example: Apple iPhone is designed in California, chip is manufactured in Taiwan and assembling is done in India or China. The paradigm shift from competitive, individualistic approach to a cooperative, interdependent one is incumbent on us as humans today. **Interdependence is** **not only the key to our happiness and well-being but** **also the key to survival.** If we fail to understand and accept this very fact it may result in various conflicts.

## Question 40(a) (Year: 2021 | Paper: GS IV) **Q4 (a). Attitude is an important component that goes as input in the development of human being. How to build a suitable attitude needed for a public servant? (10 Marks, 150 Words) Tags: Attitude: content, structure, function; its influence and relation with thought and behaviour; moral and political attitudes; social influence and persuasion.** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2021-gs4-q40-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Question** - **Introduction: Define attitude.** ## **y Body** - **Mention the importance of attitude.** - **Write how to inculcate a suitable attitude** **needed for a public servant.** - **Conclusion: Conclude on a positive note** **highlighting the notion of ‘Karmayogi** **Mission’.** **Answer:** In simple terms, **attitude can be defined as a learned** **evaluation of any aspect of the social world.** These evaluationsserveasaframeofreferenceforindividuals to understand the world around them. In the context of ethics, an individual’s attitude towards others may decide his conduct. Attitudes may or may not have moral connotations. An individual is identified more through their attitudes than the values they hold, even though the latter is equally important. It is vital as it influences overt behaviour and affects people’s views of the social and physical environment. Attitudes often define our life and their outcomes.  - It creates a difference between success and failure in various stages of life.

 - It determines interpersonal relations.

## **y For a public servant right attitude instils** - Confidence of initiative.

 - Problem solving opportunity.

 - Courage.

- **Instrumental conditioning:** One may also develop a certain attitude depending on the system of reward or punishment. Consistently rewarding someone showing respect towards the elders versus punishing him/her whenever they show disrespect will be instrumental in developing a positive attitude of respect. Example: A lion in a circus learns to stand up on a chair and jump through a hoop to receive a food treat.

- **Sociallearning:Onecanalsoobserveotherpeople** performing some actions, and by observation, they learn their conduct. For example, if a person born in secular society is more tolerant of diversity. Some agencies of socialisation are: media, peer group, educational institutes, religion, role models (great personalities), etc.

- **Institutionalising Code of Conduct:** A precise and pragmatic set of guidelines can inculcate the right attitude among public servants.

- **Team building exercises:** Team events which involve participation of public servants like district training, Bharat darshan, remote village internships help develop the right attitude of service.

- **Imbibing moral values:** Through teaching of religious texts, like Nishkama Karma of Bhagwat Geeta, etc. Recently launched **‘Karmayogi Mission’ is a step** **towardsinculcatingtherightattitudeforcivilservants.** A public servant, who is responsible for the welfare of the nation, good governance, and development of the right attitude is essential.

## Question 41(b) (Year: 2021 | Paper: GS IV) **Q4 (b). In case of crisis of conscience does emotional intelligence help to overcome the same without compromising the ethical or moral stand that you are likely to follow? Critically examine. (10 Marks, 150 Words) Tags: Emotional intelligence-concepts, and their utilities and application in administration and governance.** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2021-gs4-q41-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Question** - **Introduction: Define conscience and** **emotional intelligence.** -

## **Body** - **Write about the crisis of conscience.** - **Describe how emotional intelligence can** **help overcome crisis of conscience.** - **Conclusion: Conclude suitably.** **Answer:** Conscience is the inner sense of rightfulness and wrongfulness. It guides individual decision making to a larger extent. **Mayer and Salovey introduced the** **concept of emotional intelligence.** It can be defined as **theabilitytoperceiveandexpressemotions,assimilate** **emotions in thought, understand and reason with** **emotions and regulate emotions in oneself and** **others.** It involves understanding your own emotions, the emotions of those around you and the ability to regulate them. Thus, **Emotional intelligence (EI) is** **the ability to assess, manage, and control one’s own** **and others’ emotions.** It is critical to managing our behaviour and decision making. Conscience is an inner voice that helps in deciding what is ethical or moral. **Crisis of conscience** refers to a situation **“when someone is worrying because** **they think that they have done something unfair or** **morally wrong”.** - It occurs when there is a dilemma between two choices and an individual is compelled to take action against his or her voice of conscience.

- It is a state when an individual is going through feelings of guilt, remorse, unrest, dissonance etc. For example: The state of Arjuna in Mahabharata before Kurukshetra. **In the case of a crisis of conscience, which is an** **undesirable psychological state of mind, emotional** **intelligence can help in overcoming it without** **compromising ethical states.** - There are five elements of emotional intelligence which can enable an individual to face any crisis, such as: **self-awareness, motivation, self-** **regulation, empathy and social skills.** - Anemotionallyintelligentpersonisnota‘prisoner of his own feeling’, and has the ability to control impulses.

- Emotionally intelligent person possesses social skills, hence he has the ability of persuasion and resolving conflict. For example, for a District Magistrate crisis of conscience occurs when an order is to be executed to clear dwellings from patches of forest for wildlife conservation.

- Emotional intelligence provides courage and inner strength to act with integrity and withstand adverse situations. Emotional intelligence can help in reconciling divergent values which may cause a crisis of conscience.

- Emotional intelligence enables us to take rational stock of situations and helps in resolving crises of conscience which arise due to conflict between short term and long-term interests.

- Emotionally intelligent people are empathetic. Hence, they will stay away from causing pain and suffering to others.

- Emotionally intelligent people are self-aware and have self-control. **However, sometimes emotional intelligence may not** **be sufficient in resolving crises of conscience.** - While following professional ethics. For example: During the abortion process; the doctor, due to professional ethics, might be performing action against the moral stand.

- In absence of laws and ambiguity of laws, crisis of conscience arises in cases like surrogacy, euthanasia, etc., where individual often stands at cross-roads and faces crisis of conscience compromising moral stands.

- In the advent of scarcity of goods people purchase essential goods from black market compromising the moral stand. Despite being important, sometimes emotional intelligence may not be sufficient in resolving crises of conscience.Insuchcases,clearlaws,rules,regulations, and social values can act as a guide to ethical conduct and help to resolve crises of conduct.

## Question 42(a) (Year: 2021 | Paper: GS IV) **Q5 (a). “Refugees should not be turned back to the country where they would face persecution or human right violation.” Examine the statement with reference to ethical dimension being violated by the nation claiming to be democratic with open society. (10 Marks, 150 Words) Tags: Determinants and Consequences of Ethics in Human Interaction.** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2021-gs4-q42-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Question** - **Introduction: Give a brief overview of** **refugees and human rights violations.** ## **y Body** - **Mention ethical dilemmas associated** **with turning back the refugees.** - **Conclusion: Conclude suitably.** **Answer:** According to the **United Nations,** a **“refugee”** **is someone who is outside the country of their** **nationality** due to a well-founded fear of being **persecuted** for **reasons of race, religion, nationality,** **membership in a particular social group, or political** **opinion** and is **unable or unwilling to take advantage** **of that country’s protection.** Unquestionable rights to life, liberty, and the chance to fulfil one’s potential are known as human rights Refugees’ **fundamental human** rights are violated if they are not given shelter. Most refugees experience hardship through **no fault of their own.** Denying them the chance to flee violence and persecution goes against all moral precepts. The concept of **non-refoulement** prohibits a nation from sending a refugee back to a place where they risk persecution. However, **nations** that pride themselves on being democratic and having an open society **frequently reject refugees** and send them to their home country. **This has raised the following Ethical** ## **Dilemmas** - Countries refuse asylum citing a **lack of resources,** **dangers to national security,** and their **first duty** **towards their citizens.** The global **community’s** **obligationtoactresponsibly,however,isfrequently** **disregarded.** When it comes to dealing with refugees, nations tend to focus on the utilitarian principle (protecting their citizens) while ignoring the **deontological principle (responsibility to** **protect).** - The inalienable rights to **life, liberty, and the** **chancetofulfilone’spotentialareknownashuman** rights. Refugees are denied their fundamental human rights if they are turned away.

- The **categorical imperative of Kant** is violated when someone in need is turned away because it cannot be a universal rule. This equates to treating individuals more like means than as ends in themselves. Such conduct **fails the Gandhiji’s** **Talisman test** (action keeping in mind the interest of the weakest).

## Question 43(b) (Year: 2021 | Paper: GS IV) **Q5 (b). Should impartial and being non-partisan be considered as indispensable qualities to make a successful civil servant? Discuss with illustrations. (10 Marks, 150 Words) Tags: Impartiality and Non-Partisanship** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2021-gs4-q43-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Question** - **Introduction: Define Impartiality and Non-** **partisanship in the introduction.** ## **y Body** - **Mention why impartiality and non-** **partisanship are considered as** **indispensable qualities.** - **Conclusion: Conclude with importance of** **both.** 
**Answer:** An **ethical principle** known as **impartiality** states that judgments should be made without regard to **bias,** **prejudice, or favouring one person over another** for **inappropriate reasons.** Non-partisanship (political neutrality) is the **absence of bias toward any political** **party.** Even if a person has a strong belief in a **certain** **political idea,** this procedure involves staying out of any political party. **These two are considered as indispensable qualities** ## **for the following reasons** ## **y Impartiality** - It aids a government servant in **making** **judgments** that are based on facts rather than **biases and preconceptions.** For example, objectivity will assist the chairman of the **GST** **council,inmakingresourcerecommendations** based on **objective standards, without** **favouring** his or her own home state.

 - It **ensures civil servants** and **society/citizens** have a cordial and productive connection. For example, an **unbiased IAS/IPS** will command the respect of the people and hence will acquire their **assistance** in various **conflict** **management scenarios.** - It fosters a mindset of **equality** toward everything and everyone. For instance, impartiality will **help a local administration** execute the benefits of a **government scheme** **without caste or religious bias.** ## **y Non-partisanship** - It implies that the **civil servant** will **loyally and** **impartially** serve the **current administration.** For instance, a District Magistrate may be required to serve under federal and state governments with **different political beliefs.** - It enables a government worker to **make** **decisions** based on **constitutional principles** without fear of or favour to any political party. For instance, impartiality will help a **government officer** to perform the role of their **job effectively during elections.** - It guarantees a **productive and expert working** relationship between the **elected official and** **the government employees.** For instance, impartiality will increase or sustain **credibility** **of top government officials,** regardless of the party in power. One of the most important traits for a civil servant are **impartiality and non-partisanship.** These are required because **civil officials** are required to behave **impartially** and without bias in accordance with the **law and the Constitution.** ---

## Question 6(a) (Year: 2021 | Paper: GS IV) **Q6 (a). - It closes the accessibility gap and promotes public and administrative trust.

- Even constructive criticism is now limited by the judiciary’s contempt clause, and lawmakers are prohibited from discussing judicial behaviour. By establishing a **system of feedback for the judiciary** and aiding in the reconfiguration of the judicial process, social audit can assist in enhancing the delivery of justice.

- The **public’s expectations of the service delivery** **agency** are better understood, and the public is made more aware of the real administrative limitations. Social audit can offer perceptions that can assist in balancing the subjectivity of public opinion with the objectivity of procedures. Social audit has been extremely important, but more awareness is still needed to spread it among the general public. For social audit to work better, political and administrative cooperation is necessary. A legal foundation or a national legislation could also be taken into account.

## Question 1 (Year: 2020 | Paper: GS IV) **Q1. (a) Discuss the role of ethics and values in enhancing the following three major components of Comprehensive National Power viz. human capital, soft power (culture and policies) and social harmony. (150 Words, 10 Marks) Tag: Ethics and Human Interface: Essence, determinants and consequences of Ethics inhuman actions, ethical issues in international relations and funding.** ### Answer

It is a concept popularised by China in recent times. It says that military power alone is not sufficient for attaining strategic goals. ## **following way** - **Human Capital:** Human capital is the sum of intangible assets like, skills, education, abilities, qualities, etc of individuals or workers in a nation at a point of time that directly impact productivity. - **Social Harmony:** Social harmony is essential for a stable domestic polity and collective development. Ethicalvaluessuchasvaluingdifferences,kindness, justice, inclusion, integrity, respect for others, respect for different religious and caste groups, etc are crucial for promoting social harmony in the society. Socialharmonyholdsdecisiveimportance in the existence of world peace. Comprehensive National Power is the most important indicator in measuring the basic national conditions and resources of a country, and a comprehensive indicator of the economic, political, military and technical powers of a country. As rightly said by Dr. APJ Kalam said that “Strength respects Strength”- it is Comprehensive National Power that establishes India’s strength in the world.

- It would also include within its domain our rich cultural heritage in consonance with a futuristic outlook.

- It focuses on inculcating moral values and promoting understanding of Indian culture.

- It would create a brigade of researchers and professionals who would take our country to the great heights of development, as befitting our national aspirations.
