## Question 46 (Year: 2020 | Paper: GS IV) **Q2. (a) Hatred is destructive of a person’s wisdom and conscience that can poison a nation’s spirit. Do you agree with this view? Justify your answer. It is corrosive of a person’s wisdom and a nation’s spirit. In thecontemporaryworld,religiousviolence,communal polarisation and intolerance have increased and it is a continuous obstacle in the progress and growth of a country. One of the characteristics of hatred is the need to devalue the victim more and more. At the end the object of the hatred loses all moral or human consideration in the eyes of the hater. **Hatred severely destructs person’s wisdom and** ## **conscience as** - Hatred produces energy for destructive power and the fission that actually ignites the explosion, which is driven by the intense hostility, fear, anger or sense of injury one feels.

- Hatred reduces an individual’s quality of making good judgements or having good experience and knowledge.

- It further reduces an individual’s quality of being wise.

## **Hatred Kills Nation’s Spirit** - **Deprivation of Amenities:** Victims of intolerance are found to be deprived of facilities and opportunities, thus excluding them from contributing to the overall development of the society and subsequently lose out on selfdevelopment too.

- **Curbing of Individual Freedom:** Any form of illogical intolerance often leads to taking away individual freedom and rights. - **Destruction of Social Harmony:** Due to the communal intimidation and hatred being spread, the very fabric of a society is being diminished in the larger sense, rendering a weak and divided social strength.

 - For example, assimilation with accommodation, stable patterns of pluralism, inequality and integration etc. constitute the basic fabric of Indian society, which when taintedwithcommunalintolerancegetdivided and internally threatened.

- **Economy:** Disturbances caused due to communal intolerance largely impact the local economy due to the disruptive activities like strikes, riots, destruction of public property, etc. against each other, and also disturb the macro-economic outlookofcountriesontheglobalsphereasaresult of the apprehensions of investors or economic giants of a good work environment.

- **Political Instability:** At times massive such clashes result in political blame game, interference and unnecessary measures, projecting an unstable political atmosphere. Welfare of the nation is mainly side-lined and the representatives are caught in inconsequential issues. Hatred is a negative emotion and that is irrational and subjective. When hatred becomes all-pervasive in a society it can destroy a nation’s spirit and could further hurt social capital and cooperation among citizens. This can be explained as to how the hatred of Hitler for Jews led to the corruption of the majority of German citizens which had devastating effects on the German nation and its conscience. Therefore, hatred is indeed a serious threat to a person’s wisdom, wellbeing and national spirit. We can fight against hatred at individual level through acceptance and tolerance and at government level by restricting hate and abusive content on social media, hate political speech, caste and religious divide.

## Question 47 (Year: 2020 | Paper: GS IV) **Q2. (b) What are the main components of emotional intelligence (EI)? Can they be learned? Discuss. (150 Words, 10 Marks) Tag: Emotional intelligence-concepts, and their utilities and application in administration and governance.** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2020-gs4-q47-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Question** - **In** **Introduction,** **define** **emotional** **intelligence.** - **In Body,** - **Discuss the components of emotional** **intelligence.** - **Discuss the ways through which** **emotional intelligence can be learned.** - **Conclude, by mentioning the importance of** **emotional intelligence.** **Answer:** Emotional Intelligence(EI) is the capability of a person to assess, manage and control one’s own emotions as well as the emotions of others. It is critical to manage our behaviour and decision making. It is a scientific fact that emotions precede thought. When emotions run high, they change the way our brains function, diminishing our cognitive abilities, decision-making powers, and even interpersonal skills. Understanding and managing our emotions and the emotions of others helps us to be more successful in both our personal and professional lives.

## **Components of Emotional Intelligence** - **Self-awareness:** A person has a healthy sense of emotional intelligence and self-awareness if they understand their own strengths and weaknesses, as well as how their actions affect others. A person with emotional self-awareness is usually receptive to, and able to learn from, constructive criticism more than one who doesn’t have emotional selfawareness.

- **Self-regulation:** A person with high emotional intelligence has the ability to exercise restraint and control when expressing their emotions.

- **Motivation:** People with high emotional intelligence are self-motivated, resilient, and driven by an inner ambition rather than being influenced by outside forces, such as money or prestige.

- **Empathy:** It is an awareness of the needs and feelings of others both individually and in groups, and being able to see things from the point of view of others. An empathetic person has compassion and is able to connect with other people on an emotional level, helping them respond genuinely to other people’s concerns.

- **SocialSkill:Peoplewhoareemotionallyintelligent** are able to build trust with other people, and are able to quickly gain respect from the people they meet. We have all experienced or have met people that seem to be naturally gifted at controlling or recognizing their emotions. These individuals seem to understand how to put others in a comfortable place, or get them engaged, or even to give another person whatever it is that they need at that time. Therefore, emotional

## **intelligence can be learned through** - Workshops and Certification programs for the developmental and behavioural assessment training.

- Communication skills and the power to comprehend nonverbal cues of interaction (body language, facial expression, the tone of words, etc.).

- Group performance, especially at the workplace and maintaining a high team spirit.

- Practicing empathy by centering on verbal and non-verbal cues give invaluable insight into the feelings of colleagues or clients, which will also help in developing emotional intelligence.

- Several self-assessment training can be done too: Choosing not to retort immediately, answering wisely in conflicts, personal introspection and taking feedback from peers, friends, practicing empathy as a daily habit, etc. Emotional Intelligence training is given in most work environments, government professions throughout theglobe.Therefore,trainingthroughseveralmethods definitely help in the personal and professional growth of an individual, as any characteristic being practiced as a part of his/her general personality tends to be inculcated in the long run. As David Goleman rightly said, Emotional intelligence is a person’s ability to manage his feelings so that those feelings are expressed appropriately and effectively. This clearly means that emotional intelligence Is the largest single predictor of success.

## Question 49 (Year: 2020 | Paper: GS IV) **Q3. (b) ‘The will to power exists, but it can be tamed and be guided by rationality and principles of moral duty’. Examine this statement in the context of international relations. (150 Words, 10 Marks) Tag: Ethical issues in international relations and funding.** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2020-gs4-q49-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Question** - **In Introduction, mention the significance of** **rationality and moral duty.** - **In Body, examine the statement in detail.** - **Conclude, by mentioning the importance of** **rationality and moral duty.** **Answer:** In today’s globalised world, power is not exercised in isolation but rather it also has a moral duty attached to it. Therefore, the role of rationality and principles of moral duty is becoming more important as there has beenincreasedinteractionbetweendifferentcountries. **Rationality:** It is the quality of being in accordance with reason or logic. It cannot be arbitrary in use rather power needs to be exercised based on sound cognition and empirical evidence. For example, in international relations, there are conventions, and customary rules which guide the actions of a nation and these principles are based on rationality. Thus, a nation does not just explode a nuclear bomb based on irrationality or subjective disposition but rather is guided by the overall rationality of the action. It is the duty performed by a country **Moral Duty:** based on its moral obligation (not legally bound). The exercise of power needs to attain the basic ethical principles of equality, integrity and compassion.

- Rationality and principles of moral duty provides legitimacy or withdraws the legitimacy of the actions of governments.

 - For example: End of slavery although began withrebellionsandrevolutions,yetitsultimate demise at the global level was due to the loss of international sanction.

 - Similarly, the Soviet Union collapsed as the values that held it together were no longer credible and sustainable as the legitimacy of communism evaporated at the international level.

- There have been many attempts at forging general agreement on the composition of human rights based on the principles of rights and responsibility—thebestknownbeingtheUniversal Declaration of Human Rights as well as the United Nations Charter, the Geneva Conventions, and additional international agreement such as the Refugee Convention.

- Rationality aims at a peaceful world, respect for all and equality while forming international organizations, declarations & forums.

 - E.g.: The demand for equality in the IMF and UNOshowsthedemandforadheringtomoral and rational principles in a way.

- On various occasions rationality and moral duty have helped in avoiding ego and ideological clash between nations. For instance, the disruption between India & Pakistan relations have been avoided to surge into a full fledged war based on moral ethics.

- Aspects like global economic governance and global health governance (in pandemic) can not result in the desired results unless they are based on rationality and moral principles.

 - For instance, vaccine hoarding by developed countries like Canada is a symbol of disgrace in terms of rationality and moral duty.

- Rationalityandmoraldutyinglobalenvironmental governance seeks to ensure environmental justice on principles like “common but differentiated responsibilities”.

 - So a huge contrast can be seen between India’s earnest efforts and the USA running away from the responsibility by leaving the Paris Agreement. Thus, even though the will to power exists, on various occasions the principles of rationality and moral duty have helped in avoiding undue wars, and conflicts between nations and has helped in creating an atmosphere of mutual trust, goodwill and confidence among all the countries.

## Question 50 (Year: 2020 | Paper: GS IV) **Q4. (a) Distinguish between laws and rules. Discuss the role of ethics in formulating them. (150 Words, 10 Marks) Tag: Laws, rules, regulations and conscience as sources of ethical guidance, Codes of Ethics, Codes of Conduct.** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2020-gs4-q50-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Question** - **In Introduction, define the terms -laws and** **rules.** - **In Body,** - **Provide for a comparison between** **the two and differentiate how they are** **different from each other.** - **Explain the role of ethics in formulating** **laws and rules.** - **Conclude, by mentioning how laws and rules** **are related to ethics.** **Answer:** Laws can be defined as a system of rules enforceable by publicauthorityrequiringthemembersofacommunity to meet certain standards of conduct. Rules are codes of conduct that are designed for specific situations, similar to customs but have immense importance because there is usually a punishment related to them. Any society is governed by certain sets of laws and rules. Although in common language, these are synonyms yet there is substantial difference between

## **them, which are as follows** - **Applicability:** Laws are generally applicable for an entire country or community except the exempted areas and exempted people. Rules are applicable for any kind of society, institution, association or any activity.

- **Creation:** In a democratic country, laws are made by the elected representatives or legislatures. Rules can be set up by individuals or made by a secretary of any association, private institutions and establishments, etc.

- **Consequence:** Every citizen of a country must adhere to the laws framed by the legislature. Not adhering to laws can lead to strict and severe consequences such as imprisonment. Not following or adhering to the rules can often lead to mild consequences such as the payment of an amount as fine, suspension, etc.

- **Nature and Scope:** Laws are more strict in nature when compared with rules. Moreover, rules are broader in scope when compared to laws.

## **Role of ethics in formulating Laws and Rules** - Ethicsistheguidinglightthatdefinessoundmoral conduct and practices that ought to be followed by the individuals.

- Ethics books as a general rule of right living; especially such a rule or group of rules conceived asuniversalandunchanging.Inthiscontext,ethics form the basis of both laws and rules.

- In effective governance of the country, laws are made by the parliamentarians. These laws should be guided by ethical standards which the society and nation opt for. Ethics and laws work simultaneously to ensure that citizens act in a certain manner and also make sure that these coordinated efforts protect the health, safety and welfare of the public.

- Ensuring proper education facilities, basic healthcare facilities, employment opportunities etc, various rules and regulations should be made on the lines of ethics.

- It is ethical and necessary to provide the finances of corporate houses for the disadvantaged sections of the society. For example- Setting up of NGOs, Corporate Social Responsibility etc. Ethicshelpsinframingthecodeofconductinasociety which is essential to its functioning, for example: the Indian Constitution. In modern societies, the systems of law and rules are closely related to ethics in that they determine and enforce definite rights and duties. However, laws and rules can be neutral or they can be used to endorse ethics.

## Question 51 (Year: 2020 | Paper: GS IV) **Q4. (b) A positive attitude is considered to be an essential characteristic of a civil servant who is often required to function under extreme stress. What contributes to a positive attitude in a person? (150 Words, 10 Marks) Tag: Attitude- its influence and relation with thought and behaviour.** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2020-gs4-q51-unacademy**

Attitude can be positive or negative. Positive attitude refers to an optimistic frame of mind. According to many studies, people with this type of attitude are happier in life and are also more successful than those who do not have. A positive attitude helps you cope more easily with the daily affairs of life.

## **Positive attitude manifests in the following ways** - Positive and constructive thinking.

- This frame of mind is conducive to creative thinking – it encourages to take calculated risks, necessary for innovation.

- Motivationandenergytodothingsandaccomplish goals.

- An attitude of happiness.

## **Attributes of Positive Attitude** - Positive outlook towards society.

- Sense of service and sense of duty.

- Orientation towards the ends.

- Self awareness and self regulation.

- Knowing the philosophy from the services and organizations.

- Keeping away from negativity.

## **Factors contribute to positive attitude** - **Resilience:** It is the capacity of a person to bounce back from a tough time, adversity or difficulties. Being resilient makes a person develop positive attitudes like hope, optimism, and self-efficacy.

- **More Open Mindedness:** An attitude of open mindedness helps to turn unexpected happenings to discoveries, new knowledge to possibilities, build the capacities of acceptance and tolerance and also make a person more optimistic about the future. Therefore, bing open mindedness contributes to a positive attitude in a person.

- **Focus on Positive Things:** Focus on positive things attracts more positive situations which feed our subconscious mind positive messages; create empowering beliefs that also contribute to positive attitude in a person.

- **Social Influence:** It involves intentional and unintentional efforts to change another person’s beliefs, attitudes, or behavior. Therefore, social influence is also a contributing factor for positive attitude.

- Striving to achieve goals in life sometimes **Goals:** helps us to develop positive attitudes.

## **A few ways to reinforce positive attitudes in a person** - **Practice Gratitude:** Gratitude is a thankful appreciation for what we receive, whether tangible or intangible. We need to practice gratitude without any second thought which will ultimately reinforce a positive attitude.

- It is normal to **Retain a Sense of Purpose:** experience a sense of loss many times in life. Retaining a sense of purpose everytime we fall in life is crucial to build positive attitudes.

- **Cultivate Mindfulness:** Mindfulness is generally acquiredbytrainingthemindtofocusitsattention on the present moment in a systematic way, accepting whatever arises. This significantly helps to reinforce positive attitudes. As Winston Churchill has said, ‘attitude’ is a little thing that makes a big difference. Government must proactively work towards changing the attitude of civil servants. As civil servants are the face of any government, they are expected to play a significant role in executing the policies of the government. Nowadays, for many of the civilservants,stressisabigcontributortounhappiness and ill health. Some of the cases of suicide of civil servants have recently been seen. So, there is an urgent need to inculcate the right and positive attitude in them so that they won’t look for an escape route.

## Question 52 (Year: 2020 | Paper: GS IV) **Q5. (a) What are the main factors responsible for gender inequality in India? Discuss the contribution of Savitribai Phule in this regard? (150 Words, 10 Marks) Tag: Ethics - in private and public relationships. Human Values. Contributions of moral thinkers and philosophers from India and the world.** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2020-gs4-q52-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Question** - **In Introduction, define gender inequality.** - **In Body,** - **Factors responsible for gender** **inequality.** - **Discuss the major contributions made** **by Savitribai Phule.** - **Conclude, by mentioning how reduction in** **inequality strengthens the nation.** **Answer:** Gender inequality is an important ethical issue and is a social and cultural phenomenon in which there is discrimination against people due to their gender. Essentially, this is between men and women. This phenomenon has huge effects in all spheres of life. Gender inequality is most often seen in different areas like, work, social life, family life, etc. **The main factors responsible for gender inequality in** ## **India are** - **Illiteracy:Illiteracyisoneofthefactorsresponsible** for gender inequality. Illiteracy caused by unequal access to education and lack of sensitivity in education further leads to gender equality.

 - According to the 2011 census of India, the literacy level in India is 65.38% in which the male literacy level is 82.14% and female literacy level is only 65.46%.

- **Child Marriage:** Child specially the girls are married at a very young age. This proves fatal for their overall development and have a negative impact on their health, too.

- **Social Evils:** Social evils like dowry, sexual abuse, domestic violence, etc., are also responsible for the low status of women. These social trauma in many ways creates gender inequality.

- **Discrimination against Girl Child:** The girl child is not treated properly in the family. People still prefer the birth of a boy rather than a girl. The girl child is basically trained for household work only. Even today, female infanticide is a common social evil.

- **Participation in Decision Making:** The presence of women in the decision making sphere whether it is in the political or in the bureaucratic sphere is less.

- **Others:** Unequal investments and allocation of resources, employment opportunities in limited job creation, patriarchal system of society, etc are also the factors for gender inequality in India. All these get aggravated as political representation of females in the legislature is low. **Contribution of Savitribai Phule in promoting** ## **Gender Equality** - In 1848, she along with her husband (Jyoti Rao Phule) founded India’s first school for women at Bhide Wada in Pune. The sole motive to establish a school was to empower women through education in the 19th century.

- She established the to raise **Mahila Seva Mandal** awareness for issues concerning women’s rights.

- Shealsocalledforagatheringplaceforwomenthat was free of caste discrimination or differentiation of any kind.

- She was also an anti-infanticide activist. She opened a women’s shelter called the **Home for the** **Prevention of Infanticide,** where Brahmin widows could safely deliver their children and leave them there to be adopted if they so desired.

- She also campaigned against child marriage and was an advocate of widow remarriage.

- Savitribai and Jyoti Rao strongly opposed Sati Pratha, and they started a home for widows and forlorn children. Gender inequality is not only limited to India, it can be seen across the world. It is the degree of subjugation of women that varies throughout time and geographic areas. It is not easy to find a culture in which women have more political and social advantages than men. Therefore, it is an ethical concern for the government to reduce gender inequality in all spheres (political, economicandsocial).Reducinginequalitystrengthens economies and builds stable, resilient societies that give all individuals including boys and men the opportunity to fulfil their potential.

## Question 53 (Year: 2020 | Paper: GS IV) **Q5. (b) ‘The current internet expansion has instilled a different set of cultural values which are often in conflict with traditional values.’ Discuss. (150 Words, 10 Marks) Tag: Determinants and consequences of Ethics in-human actions; dimensions of ethics; ethics in private and public relationships.** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2020-gs4-q53-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Questions** - **In Introduction, defining culture and putting** **some light on internet expansion.** - **In Body,** - **Explain the impacts of the expansion of** **the internet on cultural values.** - **Mention the conflicting angles between** **the new value system and traditional** **value system.** - **Conclude with a fair and balanced opinion** **showing the possibility of synergy between** **traditional and internet-age values.** **Answer:** Culture is defined as shared behaviors, ideas, and artifacts that create a way of life passed from one generationtoanother.TheincreaseduseoftheInternet since the late 1990s has had a large impact on how arts and culture products such as films, books, museum exhibitions, etc., are produced, distributed, acquired, and received. Culture has significantly benefited from the invention and increased use of the internet. It moreover instilled a different set of cultural values or new values which are often seen in conflict with traditional values, as

## **discussed below** - **Materialism:** The new value system which is developing through the expansion of the internet is promoting materialism whereas the traditional value system speaks about limited consumption as consumption was not considered to be a value.

- **Self-centric or Self-centeredness:** The new value system is self-centric whereas the traditional value system was based on social consciousness. Today, social consciousness is being replaced by selfcenteredness and the internet is promoting that (E.g., indulgence with social media, etc.).

- **Eccentric Behavior:** Eccentric behavior is also attributed to expansion of the internet into rural areas. Earlier the social process was very strict, that is why all this kind of eccentric behavior was cut down but today the challenges like blue whale challenge coming into the picture.

- **Mental Health Challenges:** Nowadays, it is often seenthattheuseofinternetisleadingtotheopposite of feelings of connection, resulting in depression, anxiety, and loneliness, which are creating mental health challenges among adolescents.

- **Fragility in Relations:** More often, virtual connectedness and fragility in relations is also seen due to the rapid expansion of the internet.

- **Others:Theinternetisoftentoblameforpromoting** dissemination of false and unreliable information; radical ideologies; uncontrolled digital assaults; disrespectful, hurtful and provocative discourse; unsolicitedputtingusintouchwithothers;harmful effects on identity formation and psychological development; and cultural fragmentation. These are the antitheses of traditional values. In some areas, the expansion of internet is seen conflicting with the traditional value system but in other areas a reaffirmation of the traditional value system is seen. So, the impact of the expansion of the internet is not unilateral.

- There is a proliferation of bhakti channels on the internet which still reconfirms cultural traditional values of India.

- Along with that traditional cuisine that is also getting new life only because of the internet.

- Moreover, the age-old Yoga, Ayurveda or traditional Indian medicine, Vedic literature, etc., are getting promoted and recognized worldwide because of the expansion of the Internet. Currently,thesocialvaluesandnormsarecontinuously changing because of the influence of internet expansion. The expansion of the internet is not only conflicting with the traditional value system of India but also strengthening the traditional value system. It is also nurturing the rise of new forms of expression for art and the spread of knowledge.

## Question 1(a) (Year: 2019 | Paper: GS IV) **Q1.(a) What are the basic principles of public life? Illustrate any three of these with suitable examples. (150 Words, 10 Marks) Tag: Essence, determinants and consequences of Ethics in-human actions; Ethics - in private and public relationships.** ### Answer

## **Decoding the Question** - **In Introduction, try to elaborate the meaning** **of public life.** - **In Body, discuss a few basic principles of** **public life with examples.** - **Conclude, with an opinion.** **Answer:** Public life is an aspect of social life. Citizens in a public life should be bound together neither by feelings nor by self-interest but by a sincere commitment to common values discovered by public reason — values such as political freedom, solidarity, shared traditions and cultural heritage. As a public representative there are some qualities and traits that need to be cultivated by a person. For these qualities to develop, certain principles are required.

## **Basic principles of public life** - **Selflessness:** It is characterized by low levels of self-centeredness and a low degree of importance given to the self. Holders of public office should take decisions solely in terms of the public interest. They should not do so in order to gain financial or other material benefits for themselves, their family, or their friends.

- **Example:** A municipal representative should not hesitatetogetridofanyillegalreligiousstructureif it is creating inconvenience to the public. Securing votes by not taking any action will only make the representative a selfish personality.

- **Honesty:** Holders of public office have an obligation to declare any private interests relating to their public duties and to take remedial steps to resolve any conflicts arising in a way that protects the public interest.

- **Example:Apublicofficialisapproachedbyashady** builder, who promises to give a large amount of money if he is given permission to construct an unnecessary footpath. Honest representatives will not give any heed to such offers.

- Ashok Khemka, an IAS officer who has been transferred about 50 times in his career due to his honesty towards service.

- **Responsibility:** A public official is expected to be responsible for the task assigned and take initiative to fight for the rights of citizens. Also, he/ she is expected to show his exemplary leadership qualities to tackle any sort of crisis.

- **Example:** Public representatives should lead a campaign for creating awareness among the people regarding the importance of maintaining cleanliness. This initiative can be successful only if it has a suitable person who takes responsibility to carry it out.

## **Other principles** - **Integrity:** It refers to honesty or trustworthiness in the discharge of official duties. All the holders of public office should not place themselves under any obligation, financial or otherwise, outside individuals/organizations that might influence them in the performance of their official duties.

- **Transparency:** For the proper functioning of the administrative machinery it is imperative that the process is transparent, barring the conditions of nation’s security where secrecy is required, so that all the stakeholders have confidence and trust in the system.

- **Objectivity:** In public service, including making government appointments, awarding contracts, or referring individuals for rewards and benefits, holders of public office should make selections on merit. When people opts for a public life, they must follow ethics distinct from private morality. The principles must be allowed to develop in those associated with publiclifethroughpropertrainingandguidance.Thus, we can observe the importance of certain qualities that can make a difference in the lives of individuals in society, if implemented properly.

[Question ID: 2020-gs4-q57-unacademy]

## Question 58(b) (Year: 2019 | Paper: GS IV) **Q1.(b) What do you understand by the term ‘public servant’? Reflect on the expected role of a public servant? (150 Words, 10 Marks) Tag: Concept of public service.** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2019-gs4-q58-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Question** - **In Introduction, define public servant.** - **In Body,** - **Mention the characteristics of a public** **servant.** - **Discuss the expected role of a public** **servant.** - **Mention a few notable works by public** **servants.** - **Discuss the importance of public servants.** **Answer:** Public servants refers to any person employed by the government either directly through appointment or indirectly through election. Judges, bureaucrats, elected representatives, they all are public servants and provide services to the nation. In other words, a public servant is a person who works for the government (Centre, State, and Local) such as a teacher, administrators, police officers, Judge etc to serve the interests of citizens. A civil servant is someone who values public good over her individual interests in his official capacity.

## **Characteristic of Public Servant** - **Selflessness:** The public servant has to keep welfare of the public and society above his/her own advantage.

- **Empathetic:** The public servant should be able to understand the feelings and emotions of the public for creating a positive impact through his decisions and actions.

- **Moral integrity:** The civil servant should hold high morals and ethical beliefs while performing his role. **Expected role of a public servant:** The expected role of a public servant depends on the sector in which one is working. For example: as a teacher of public school, one should try to impart best possible education to students without any discrimination. As a doctor in a government hospital, one must treat his patients well and to the best of capacity. In a broader sense, Being a public servant they are expected to play certain role

## **which are as follows** - **Resource Management:** A public servant has to deal with the management of resources that are publiclyownedinawaythatenhancespublictrust.

- **Policy Formulation:** A public servant is expected to formulate policies (if necessary) at their own spheres for better administration.

- **Regulation and Law Enforcement:** A public servant is responsible for better regulation of public and other bodies, guided by the regulatory framework. Moreover, proper enforcement of central and state laws are also expected from their end.

- **CatalyticAgent:Itisexpectedthatapublicservant** should act as a catalytic agent for the development of the nation and promote socio-economic wellbeing and justice.

- **Good Governance:** It is also expected that a public servant should promote good governance through timely response, efficient delivery of public services, solving public grievances, etc.

- **Inculcate Best Practices:** A public servant should inculcate international best practices in service delivery for the betterment of the society and a nation.

- **Other:** A public servant while performing his role should exercise certain values like accountability, commitment to public service, integrity, honesty, effective implementation of policy, leadership, compassion towards the vulnerable sections in their public transactions.

## **A Few Notable Works by Public Servants** - Armstrong Pame, an IAS officer from the Zeliangrong community (Naga tribes), an empath bynature.Hetookonthemissionofimprovingthe lives of the people in Manipur. He crowd sourced was successful in building a 100 kms long road in a remote hill of Manipur without government fund.

- Judges like J.S.Verma worked till age of 80 to prepare report after Nirbhaya rape case.

- P Narahari, he is known for the change that he brought in the life of people with disabilities. He constructed and advocated for a barrierfree environment that ensures that people with disabilities can move about safely and independently. Public servants are the backbone of any governance system. Hence, they should meet the highest level of service delivery. When we count such big names, we must not forget the contribution of millions of public servants working in different capacities in different partsofthecountrythatmakethesystemrun.Without the dedication and selflessness of people manning the public institutions at meagre wages compared to the private sector, large people like India cannot work.

## Question 59 (Year: 2019 | Paper: GS IV) **Q2. (a) Effective utilization of public funds is crucial tomeetdevelopmentgoals.Criticallyexaminethe reasons for under-utilization and mis-utilization of public funds and their implications. (150 Words, 10 Marks) Tag: Utilization of public funds** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2019-gs4-q59-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Question** - **In Introduction, explain public funds.** - **In Body,** - **Explain the reasons for under-** **utilizationand mis-utilization of public** **funds.** - **Discuss the implications.** - **Conclude, by mentioning the consequences.** **Answer:** Publicfunds can be referred to as revenue raised by the government through taxation, fines and borrowings or external financing to meet various expenditure needs for the development of the nation. Effective utilization of public funds is critical to meeting development goals. There is a weak correlation between increased in public spending and the achievement of development outcomes in most developing countries. Government ineffectiveness in the form of waste, inefficiency and corruption is largely responsible. **Reasons for under-utilization and mis-utilization of** ## **public funds** - **Corruption:** The huge sum of money earmarked for public activities are snatched by officials and politicians in form of bribes. Consequently, funds are not enough to contribute towards development. Example: Money allocated for construction of houses for the poor is consumed by corrupt officials.

- **Systemic Weaknesses:** It demonstrated as shortage of trained, regular staff for various important roles like programme management, finance or accounts and frontline service provision; this contributed to weakening the capailities of the government machinery in the States for implementation of Plan schemes.

- **PoliticalRivalry:Sometimespoliticalclassindulges** in acts of vendetta where they do not cooperate in the release of funds to their opposition. Example: The Government in power not allocating required developmental funds to opposition MLAs.

- **Diversion:** The funds allocated for one activity is diverted to another in order to meet strict control over finances. For Example: Funds allocated to road repair are diverted for freebies.

- **Red Tapism:** Colonial bureaucratic attitudes sometimes acts as hindrance in carrying out developmental tasks. This complicates the process due to which funds are not properly utilized.

- **Weakly Performing Public Institutions:** Weaklyperforming public institutions can seldom be expected to reform themselves in the absence of external pressure. Unlike private companies, public organisations face no direct competitive pressures, and political systems are often inadequate at mobilizing the public pressure for specific institutional reform.

- **Other:** Bottlenecks in budgetary processes in the schemes, such as delay in the flow of funds, in releasing sanction orders for spending, etc. Moreover, lack of need-based budgeting in various schemes implies allocations for some of the schemes being decided in a top-down and unrealistic manner.

## **Implications of Public Funds** - **Public apathy:** The public suffer due to underutilization and mis-utilization of funds. The rightful fund does not result in any welfare for society.

- **Rise of extremism:** The problems of Naxalism, Maoism are a product of such apathy shown by the authorities. The funds, even though allocated by the government, do not reach people.

- **Instability:** This cycle of vendetta politics results in stalling of political progress in the society. The political blame game results in only unstable conditions but does not prove fruitful for the society. Underutilisation and misutilisation of public funds leads to blockage in the operations of various planned schemes and projects. This also compromises with the quality of work done and service rendered to the public. Moreover, in the long run, this blooms into an unjust development scenario in which all do not reap the benefits of development equally. There may be wastage of public money or leakage by a particular section. It will erode the legitimacy as well as the capacity of the state to rule.

## Question 60(b) (Year: 2019 | Paper: GS IV) **Q2.(b) “Non-performance of duty by a public servant is a form of corruption”. Do you agree with this view? Justify your answer. (150 Words, 10 Words)** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2019-gs4-q60-unacademy**

## **Tag** ## **Decoding the Question** - **In Introduction, elaborate the definition of** **corruption.** - **In Body,** - **Write a few points in favour of this view** **(also give some examples).** - **Write a few points against the view.** - **Conclude, with an opinion.** **Answer:** Corruption is one of the most prevalent ethical issues with which public servants are confronted. Transparency International considers corruption as abuse of power which erodes social fabric. Corruption undermines public trust in the political system, institutions and leadership. Non-performance of one’s duty is regarded as next to corruption depending upon the situation and intent. But one cannot discern just the non-performance of duty as corruption without analysing its causes.

## **Non-performance of duty as a form of corruption** - **Acting Against the Order:** A public servant is expected to follow the rules and regulations. It is necessary to do as per the instructions in order to keep the credibility of the institution intact. Failing to follow instructions can be compared to corrupt practice.

 - Example, a public servant, when ordered to evict illegal construction and failing to do so, will create a common perception that the official has taken a bribe to avoid eviction.

- **Misleading Public:** Not doing duty is like hiding information from the public domain. Such non performance is creating a sense of misleading acts that is equal to corruption. For example, a public servant fails to clear the pension of a citizen leading to failure to perform his duty.

- **Unprofessional:** Professionalism is one of the major features of a public official and one needs to maintain it at all levels. Failing to keep up with workexpectationsmaybeconsideredtobecorrupt practice. Example, a police officer avoiding to reach the site of the crime deliberately.

- **Violating Basic Rights:** All civil servants are entrusted with public duty for the welfare of the masses. Negligence to the public duty costs masses by the loss of their freedom, health, education, rights and even life. Hence, non-performance of dutybyapublicofficialisalsoaformofcorruption.

- Example, a doctor willfully failing to hospital on time threatens the life of the patients or a teacher avoiding his duty not only endangers the future of children but of society as a whole.

- **Resource Wastage :** It is the duty of all public servants to ensure that the public money is spent most efficiently and that programs are provided effectively, without discrimination or prejudice, with transparency and without waste of money or resources. **Non-performance of duty is not always consider as a** ## **form corruption** - **Technical Issues:** Unintentional situations may arise that may prevent an official from performing his or her duty. This does not mean that a public servant is practicing corruption. For example, connectivity problems may prevent an official from doing his/her duty.

- **Inner-consciousness:** Sometimes the duty a public official is expected to perform may not be ethically right. In such cases the official may decide against doing his duty. For example, public servants may refuse to follow some orders as it may harm community interests.

- **Other Issues:** Non-performance of duty by a public servant may be due to lack of coordination, lack of human power and budget. A civil servant should use his office that derives rightfulness from the public and utilizes public resources to serve public interests only. Whenever public servants act in ways that violate set standards for public servants, they violate their natural order of things that hold all together. That deviation from duty itself is what is enough to make one corrupt and not what fruits or pains that deviations bring to one. However, in all cases not performing duty does not alwaystranslatetocorruption.Butnotperformingthem deliberately without a valid reason can be considered a grave offence. Hence, it is essential for every public servant to perform their duty as mandated in order to uphold the constitutional values and become a vehicle for change in the life of the masses.

## Question 61 (Year: 2019 | Paper: GS IV) **Q3. (b) What is meant by “crisis of conscience”? How does it manifest itself in the public domain? (150 Words, 10 Marks) Tag: Ethical concerns and dilemmas in government and private institutions; laws, rules, regulations and conscience as sources of ethical guidance.** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2019-gs4-q61-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Question** - **InIntroduction,define “crisisofconscience”.** - **In Body, explain how it manifests itself in the** **public domain.** - **Conclude, by mentioning a relevant quote.** **Answer:** Conscience is a person’s moral sense of right and wrong, viewed as a guide to one’s behaviour. It is often referred to as the inner voice of reason. This inner voice may or may not involve elaborate reasoning but it does concern with judging a situation as good or bad i.e. a moral choice is involved. Conscience can also be suppressed willingly. A person may choose to do something which his/her conscience opposes because there is some higher authority or a threat prohibiting the desired action. This leads to a crisis of conscience. Crisisofconsciencecanbesimplydefinedasasituation wherein people will have to choose between among their inner morals and their duty or responsibility. It is an ethical dilemma that is especially prevalent among honest and upright public servants.

## **Crisis of conscience in Public Life** - **Dilemas:** It is a situation in which a difficult choice has to be made between two or more alternatives. Public officials face a crisis of conscience in the form of ethical dilemas where they have to choose between two competeing alternatioves, which are valid in their own sphere. It becomes interesting to see whether an official chooses his inner conscience or his duty.

- Example: An IAS officer posted as SDM in a district has been ordered by the government to forcefully evict squatters on government land. The official finds that the occupants are poor people who have small infants to take care of. In such cases the official needs to decide between his/her conscience of giving them time to vacate or go by strict rulebook order and perform his duty.

- **Public Activism:** It is the use of direct action, such as a demonstration or strike, in opposition to or support of a cause. A public servant is forced to resort to public activism when he/she is unable to do what his conscience expects. In this case he can take a path of political activism or social activism.

- Example: An officer is trying hard to sanction a drinking water project for a residential area with poor population but there is ignorance on the part of concerned officials. He/she is forced to file a petition with court in order to implement the project.

- **Whistle Blowing:** It is the disclosure by a person, generally an employee, to the public or to those in authority, of mismanagement, corruption and illegality. ‘Crisis of conscience’ and its effect on individuals is visible in the form of ‘whistle blowing’, where an individual throws light over irregularities occurring inside the organisation out into the public domain.

- Example: Public official in the revenue department observes that some colleagues have conspired to illegally sell government land which has been allocated to an orphanage. He is also been offered huge amount of money to stay quiet. Here, ‘Crisis of conscience’ will force the official to bring out this information into the public domain, even if it is anonymously.

- **Other Examples:** If a situation arises in which a work order has to be given and a public servant friend has also applied for it. And is somewhat indirectly referring to it,to give the order to him without looking Into the other tenders. Though he is honest in nature, his crisis of conscience would manifest itself. If any individual is facing a ‘crisis of conscience’, a sudden behavioural change can be seen. He/she may feel remorse for his/her action that may lead to depression or in extreme cases a suicidal tendency. ThequotefromtheBaha’i(world’snewestindependent global belief system,teaches the oneness of God, the unity of humanity , and the essential harmony of religion) teachings gives us three clear, specific guidelines for dealing with any crisis of conscience which are: Equity and Justice; Mercy and kindness; and Treat compassionately all humankind.

[Question ID: 2019-gs4-q61-unacademy]

## Question 62(a) (Year: 2019 | Paper: GS IV) **Q3.(a) What is meant by the term ‘constitutional morality’? How does one uphold constitutional morality? (150 Words, 10 Marks)** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2019-gs4-q62-unacademy**

## **Tag** ## **Decoding the Question** - **In Introduction, define Constitutional** **morality.** - **In Body,** - **Write a few points about Constitutional** **morality with example.** - **Explain how Constitutional morality** **can be upheld.** - **Conclude, by mentioning the importance of** **Constitutional morality.** **Answer:** According to Dr. B. R. Ambedkar, Constitutional morality means an effective coordination between conflicting interests of people and the administrative cooperationtoresolvethemwithoutanyconfrontation amongst the various groups working to the realization of their ends. The idea of Constitutional morality has been regarded as a paramount reverence for the constitution. It provides us a principled understanding for unfolding the work of governance. It mentions norms for institutions to survive and an expectation of conduct thatwillmeetboththetextandtheConstitutionalsoul. It makes the governing institutions and representatives accountable.

## **Constitutional morality** - Constitutional morality involves adherence to Constitutional principles of democracy like, commitment to liberty, Constitutional supremacy, parliamentary form of government and self restraint, rule of law, equality, etc.

- Example: In Supreme Court’s Sabarimala verdict religious freedom, gender equality and the right of women to worship guaranteed under Article 14, 21 and 25 of the Constitution was reinstated which struck down the practice of banning entry ofwomenofacertainagetotheSabarimalatemple in Kerala as unconstitutional.

- Constitutional morality here went against social morality that discriminates against women based on biological reasons like menstruation.

## **Constitutional morality can be uphold by** - Criticising and raising voice against the unconstitutional practices. It is necessary to speak up against any action, which is illegal or unethical under the Constitution.

- Letting Constitutional morality guide the judicial decisions instead of popular morality, while interpreting the Constitution, Constitutional morality is being upheld.

- Only our beliefs and actions for upholding constitutional values are not enough. Our moral obligation is to educate the public regarding the importance of uphold these ideals. This will be fruitful for our democracy in the long run.

- Ensuring a commitment to the values like Constitutional supremacy, rule of law, equality, liberty,selfrestraintandintoleranceforcorruption etc.

- Locating the content matter and contours of Constitutional morality through debates and discussions so that it is not being ignorantly and dangerously used in courts. Constitutional morality as a governing ideal that reflects the need to preserve the public trust in the democratic institutions. It lets people cooperate and coordinate to pursue constitutional aspirations that cannot be achieved single-handedly. Constitutional morality is significant for constitutional laws to be effective in implementation. Without constitutional morality, the operations of a constitution tends to become arbitrary.

## Question 63(a) (Year: 2019 | Paper: GS IV) **Q4.(a) Explain the basic principles of Citizen’s Charter movement and bring out its importance. (150 Words, 10 Marks) Tag: Citizen’s Charters** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2019-gs4-q63-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Question** - **In Introduction, try to define Citizen’s** **Charter.** - **In Body,** - **Mention the components of Citizen’s** **Charters.** - **Discuss its basic principles.** - **Explain the importance of Citizen’s** **Charters.** - **Try to conclude by mentioning the need of** **increasing the scope of Citizen’s Charter.** **Answer:** TheCitizen’scharterinitiativeisaresponsetothequest for solving the problems which a citizen encounters. Theconceptofacitizen’scharterenrishestrustbetween serviceprovidersanditsusers.Itwasfirstimplemented in the United Kingdom as a national programme with an objective to continuously improve the quality of public services. The objective of a citizen’s charter is to tap the gap between citizens and administration so as to streamline administration in tune with the needs and concerns of citizens. In India, the idea of a citizen’s charter was adopted at the ‘1997-Conference of Chief Ministers of various States and Union Territories’. **Citizen’s Charters in India has the following** ## **components** - Vision and mission statement of the organization.

- Details of business transacted by the organization.

- Details of clients.

- Details of grievance redressal mechanism.

- Statementsofservicesincludingstandards,quality, timeframe, etc. **Basic Principles of Citizen’s Charters:** There are six basic principles of Citizen’s Charter

## **movement are** - **Quality:** Improving the quality of services;

- **Choice:** Wherever possible must be allowed;

- **Standards:** Specifying expectations and how to act if standards are not met;

- **Value:** Value for the taxpayers’ money;

- **Accountability:** Accountability of individuals and organisations; and

- **Transparency:** Transparency in rules, procedures, schemes and grievances.

## **Importance of Citizen’s Charters** - It boosts accountability of public institutions and authorities in the delivery of various public services.

- It improves good governance and also ameliorates the effectiveness of organizations by having measurable standards.

- It enhances the quality of services delivered by incorporating an internal and external monitoring entity.

- Being citizen-centric, it creates a professional and customer-oriented environment for the delivery of services.

- It helps boost the staff morale and also enhances transparency and openness. ACitizen’sCharterisanexpressionofanunderstanding betweencitizensandapublicserviceprovidedinregard to the quantity and quality of services which citizens receive in exchange for their taxes. As of now Citizen’s Charter is mere a written, voluntary declaration by service providers about service standards, choice, accessibility, non-discrimination, transparency and accountability but it should be an effective tool to realize transparency and accountability for good governance.

## Question 64(b) (Year: 2019 | Paper: GS IV) **Q4.(b) There is a view that the Official Secrets Act is an obstacle to the implementation of the Right to Information Act. Do you agree with this view? Discuss. (150 Words, 10 Marks) Tag: Information sharing and transparency in government, Right to Information.** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2019-gs4-q64-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Question** - **In Introduction, try to mention a recent case.** - **In Body,** - **Mention some provisions.** - **Explain how OSA is an obstacle to the** **implementation of RTI Act.** - **Write the way forwards.** - **Try to conclude with a balanced opinion.** **Answer:** Thegovernmenthassoughtactionagainstanewspaper and news agency under Official Secrets Act, 1923 for publishing classified documents related to India’s deal to buy 36 Rafael jets from France. However, the Judiciarymade it crystal clear and dispelled the doubts many of us have regarding the Officials Secrets Act (OSA) being an obstacle in effective implementation of the Right to Information Act. Following are the provisions about the interactions of

## **OSA and RTI Act potential conflict** - In case of conflict between these two laws, the provisions of the RTI Act will override those of the OSA.

- The Section 22 of the RTI Act states that its provisions will have effect despite anything that is inconsistent with them in the OSA.

- Under the Section 8(2) of the RTI Act, a public authority may allow access to information covered under the OSA, “if the public interest in disclosure outweighs the harm to the protected interests”.

- RTI under Section 24, mandates even security and intelligence organisations to disclose information on corruption and human rights violations. It is clear that OSA is not an obstacle as in case of conflict, it is clear to give way to RTI. Nonetheless, there are several other issues that do not allow for effective implementation of the RTI Act, which are as

## **follows** - **Protects Corrupt Officials:** The officials, who are responsible for providing information under RTI circumvent the files from reaching public domain by quoting Official Secrets Act. This keeps their corrupt activities concealed.

- **As Measure of Threat:** The government may use the Official Secrets Act to threaten activists who have collected important information under the RTI Act. This can deject RTI activists from carrying out their duties.

- **Culture of secrecy:** The repeated use and misuse made confidentiality a norm hinders the very essenceofRTIAct.OSAalongwithotherrulesand guidelines impinge on the freedom of information as they have developed a culture of secrecy, which is against the essence of the Right to Information.

- **Ambiguity:** The language and words of the law are ambiguous making it a legal provision sheltering issues of governance into a confidential matter. The information can be classified as secret under Section 5 of OSA. The term ‘secret’ has not been defined in the Act. Therefore, in the absence of definition public officials enjoy the discretion to classify anything as ‘secret’.

- **No Change in Ambiguous Clauses:** The OSA was enacted during the colonial era. Over time law has not been changed to improve its provisions. Still under RTI, OSA is a cause of exemption and no amendment is done to define the ‘secret’ under the act.

## **Way Forward** - **National Security Act:** The 2nd ARC Report had suggested that the Act should be substituted by a chapter in the National Security Act (NSA). The NSA should incorporate the necessary aspects of defined secrecy, as it had become a contentious issue after the implementation of the RTI Act. The Law Commission of India also suggested consolidation of all legislation dealing with national security into a single law and passing the National Security Act.

- **Defining Security:** Section 5 of OSA may be amended to make the penal provisions of OSA applicableforviolationsaffectingnationalsecurity. The ‘security’ clause under the section should be defined clearly with sufficient details.

- **Freedom of Speech and Expression:** The Supreme Court suggested, the right to freedom of speech & expression and information should override the archaic OSA. The Court ruled that the protections to the whistleblowers should be provided to make sure that those who expose corruption are also safe. The Official Secret Act indeed protects sensitive information (expenditure for spying agencies, deployment of nuclear weapons, etc) and discourage unethical practices (stealing sensitive information and sellingitintheopenmarket)butatthesametimeitalso hinders the very essence of RTI. Therefore, after the enactment of the RTI, OSA needs to be reconsidered. Both the Acts have different objectives but should complement each other and not be a hindrance in good governance.

[Question ID: 2019-gs4-q64-unacademy]

## Question 65(a) (Year: 2019 | Paper: GS IV) **Q5.(a) What do you understand by probity in governance? Based on your understanding of the term, suggest measures for ensuring probity in government. (150 Words, 10 Marks) Tag: Concept of public service; Philosophical basis of governance and probity; Quality of service delivery, Utilization of public funds, challenges of corruption.** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2019-gs4-q65-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Question** - **In Introduction, define probity in** **governance.** - **In Body,** - **Discuss the importance of probity in** **governance.** - **Explain the necessary measures for** **ensuring probity in governance.** - **Conclude, by a balanced opinion.** **Answer:** Probity is the quality of consistently having strong moral principles and strictly following them. It includes principles such as uprightness, honesty, integrity, transparency and incorruptibility. ProbityinGovernanceisrelatedwiththeproprietyand character of various government agencies. Accordding to the National Commission to Review the working of Constitution’s (NCRWC, 2002) report on ‘Probity in governance’, an important prerequisite for ensuring probity in governance is absence of corruption. The other requirements are effective laws, rules and regulations governing every aspect of public life and, more important, an effective and fair implementation of those laws, etc. Thus it involves adopting an ethical and transparent approach, allowing the process to withstand scrutiny.

## **Importance of Probity in Governance** - It leads to wise and ethical outcomes and building of trust over time.

- It provides an objective and independent view on the fairness in the process.

- Avoids of sub-optimal outcomes, corruption and poor perception.

- It helps in checking the misuse and abuse of power by various government agencies such as magistracy, police and other public service providers of e.g. PWD, health, education, etc.

- It is an important and vital requirement for an efficient and effective system of administration and for socio-economic development.

## **Steps to ensure probity in governance are** - Need to promote e-governance (through the use of Information and Communication Technology) for transparency. It would also help in anonymous complaints against public servants by ordinary people.

- Need to work on behavioral change in bureaucrats through training, performance appraisal, inculcationofvalueslikeempathyandcompassion.

- Need for a law providing for confiscation of illegally acquired assets of public servants.

- Need to strengthen the criminal justice system.

- Enactment of the Public Interest Disclosure Act – to strengthen the Whistleblower’s Protection Act and RTI Act etc are also necessary.

- Senseofdisciplineneedstobeinstilledbythehead of organisations and leaders of society. A mix of adequate rules and regulations, creating favorable administrative processes and development of ethical competence is desirable to ensure probity in governance. Excessive dependence must not be on external mechanisms because it always keeps a prospect to breach but focus must be to create men of strength and character who uphold such values on their own. Though imparting a sense of morality in the people is a long-term process which needs immediate attention, some legislatives ways can be designed with an utmost urgencytoensureprobityingovernance.Thus,Probity in governance is an uncompromised requirement for an efficient and effective system of governance and for socio economic development.

## Question 66(b) (Year: 2019 | Paper: GS IV) **Q5.(b) “Emotional Intelligence is the ability to make your emotion work for you instead of against you.” Do you agree with this view? Discuss. (150 Words, 10 Marks) Tag: Emotional intelligence-concepts, and their utilities and application in administration and governance.** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2019-gs4-q66-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Question** - **In** **Introduction,** **define** **emotional** **intelligence.** - **In Body,** - **Discuss the traits of emotionally** **intelligent leaders.** - **Mention the ways to make decisions in** **favour of our own decision making.** - **Conclude, by an opinion.** **Answer:** Emotional intelligence is the capability of a person to assess, manage and control one’s own emotions as well as the emotions of others. It is critical to manage our behaviour and decision making. It is a scientific fact that emotions precede thought. When emotions run high, they change the way our brain’s function, diminishing our cognitive abilities, decision-making powers, and even interpersonal skills. Understanding and managing our emotions and the emotions of others helps us to be more successful in both our personal and professional lives.

## **Traits of emotionally intelligent administrators** - **High Self-regard:** Good administrators have high self-regard. They know their strengths and capitalize on those strengths, as well as know their shortcomings and fill the gaps with people with strong skills in such areas.

- **Maintain Balance in Life:** Good administrators maintainbalanceintheirpersonalandprofessional lives. They avoid burning out by managing their time well.

- **Inspire a Shared Vision:** As an administrator, he/ she must convince others that he/she understands what others need and have their best interests at heart. Inspiring a shared vision requires empathy and optimism for it gives our vision a positive and desirable essence. Empathy ensures that we select the right path in terms of what others want to see and hear from us.

- **Enable Others to Act:** They enable others by fostering collaboration and building trust. Successful administrators share power, delegate well, and do what’s necessary to help others perform better. There is a need for self-regard and interpersonal skills to render others to act. To build successful relationships, one needs the skills to engage and relate to others in a meaningful way.

- **Challenge the Process:** An emotionally intelligent administrator strives for a good change. He looks for opportunities to grow and also to experiment and take risks. One of the key emotional intelligence skills that are needed in order to challenge the status quo is flexibility. It is fexible people are more likely to try new things, take risks, and face new challenges without fear. Peoplewhoareemotionallyintelligentdonoteliminate allemotionsfrom theirdecision-making process.They remove only those emotions that have nothing to do with the decision or desruptive for the process. Decisions, especially involving risk, are often guided by emotions, such as anxiety, that in fact emerge from completely unrelated events. Emotionally intelligent administrators are less likely to make a mistake with “incidental” anxiety because they recognize the irrelevant source of their emotions. Ways to make emotions work in favour in your decision making: Recognize and name all feelings you are experiencing in connection with the decision.

- Bring your feelings inward to identify its root cause (an emotion).

- Process that emotion, not one of its symptoms (a feeling).

- Be aware of whether you want to make a decision from this specific emotion or if you want to adjust the course. Of course, you also need to do all the usual things you frequently hear about as conducive to objective decision making, such as avoiding making decisions when you are tired, stressed or being influenced by non-objective actors. Nevertheless, identifying the root or emotional basis of your feelings will go a long way toward improving your decision making.

## Question 71(a) (Year: 2018 | Paper: GS IV) **(a) State the three basic values, universal in nature, in the context of civil services and bring out their importance. (150 Words, 10 Marks) Tag: Status and problems; ethical concerns and dilemmas in government and private institutions; accountability and ethical governance; strengthening of ethical and moral values in governance.** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2018-gs4-q71-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Question** - **In Introduction, try to define values.** - **In Body,** - **Discuss three basic values that are** **universal in nature.** - **Explain their importance.** - **In Conclusion, write your opinion.** **Answer:** Values are the belief of individuals that drives human actions or human behavior. Values like, honesty, truthfulness, sincerity, kindness, etc., are the essential components of an organizational culture and are instrumental in determining, guiding and informing behaviour. For bureaucrats, adherence to high-level public service values is crucial to generate substantial public trust and confidence. In certain circumstances, making ethical decisions is required to weigh values against each other and choosing which values to elevate. Moreover, when people have different values it mayresultinconflict,clashofreferencesandpriorities.

## **The three basic values, universal in nature are** - **Integrity and Honesty:** Integrity is the quality of being honest and having strong moral principles. It is about putting the obligations of public service above your own personal interests.

 - Public interest alone should concern civil servants in their official decision making and not by any financial or other consideration .

- **Courage:** Courage is the resistance against any opposition by challengers. This quality helps in fighting against fear.

- **Impartiality and Political Neutrality:** Impartiality ensures equality without any bias and prejudices in the general, non-partisanship ensures a neutral approachinpoliticsandasolidcommitmenttothe government. Civil servants in carrying out their official work, like granting project permission, granting licenses, public goods procurement,etc., need to remain impartial. Moreover, their decisions should be based on merit and free from any partisan/political consideration.

## **Other Basic Values** - **Commitment to Public Service:** A Civil servant should deliver services in a effective, fair and impartial and courteous manner to serve the public interest always.

## **Importance of these values** ## **y Integrity and Honesty** - These will prevent the official from falling prey to corrupt practices in lieu of money or any other temptations.

 - These will help in ensuring that official duties are performed properly because there will be no reason for delaying the act.

## **y Courage for Public Service** - A civil servant is expected to be fearless when carrying out his duties in the correct manner. However,insomecasestheremaybehesitancy due to external pressure. It is courage helps to overcome challenges.

 - As a public servant, an individual has to live and work in a challenging environment far from their comfort zone. Courage will help in sustaining the confidence of the public servant.

## **y Impartiality** - It is necessary for a civil servant to be politically neutral when it comes to taking decisions because his/her decisions have a large impact on the society. By being impartial an official ensures that correct action has been taken without getting influenced by external pressure.

 - Impartial nature averts immoral acts. Integrity, dedication to public service, impartiality, political neutrality, anonymity, accountability, devotion to duty, exemplary behaviour etc. are said to be the hallmarks of an efficient civil service. Thus, their relevance exists in all conditions and situations. This will act as a beacon of light for civil servants in their future endeavours.

## Question 72(b) (Year: 2018 | Paper: GS IV) **(b) Distinguish between “Code of ethics” and “Code of conduct” with suitable examples. (150 Words, 10 Marks) Tag: Code of ethics, Code of conduct.** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2018-gs4-q72-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Question** - **In Introduction, try to mention the context.** - **In Body,** - **Explain code of ethics and code of** **conduct with examples.** - **Write key differences.** - **In Conclusion, mention the significance and** **need for the enactment.** **Answer:** A key element of sound administration is the development, issuance and acceptance of a comprehensive Code of Ethics and Code of Conduct, which sets out the minimum standards of behaviour required of all civil servants. These standards further serve as a guide when making decisions and taking actions. The regulation of Conduct asserts some specific actions are appropriate, others inappropriate. With similarities, comes differences. The key elements of such Codes are: Personal Responsibility, compliance with the law, relations with the public, limitations on the acceptance of Gifts, Rewards, Hospitality and Discounts, etc.

## **Code of Ethics** - It contains a declaration of values or set of written guidelines for the civil servants. The code is like the Constitution with general principles to guide behaviour, outlining a set of principles that affect decision-making. It includes the principles of integrity, impartiality, commitment to public service, accountability, etc.

- When faced with ethical dilemmas or debatable situations, the codes articulated in the Code of Ethics can help guide decision making.

- **Example of Code of Ethics:** Civil servants are committed to public services. The Code of Ethics determines that decisions of the public servant should be guided by larger common good and to choose the most beneficial solution for the community.

 - Several attempts have been made in the year 1997 by the Department of Administrative Reforms and in 2006 by the Department of Personnel to prepare Code of Ethics with the object of developing public services as a professional, politically neutral, merit based and accountable civil service, but the Public Service Bill seems to have gone in cold storage.

## **Code of Conduct** - Code of conducts are the set of norms or rules originated from the Code of Ethics. It enlists the rules into specific guidelines that must be followed by the members of the organisation.

- It sets out the standards of behaviour expected of those working as the public servant. The Civil Service code outlines the core values of Civil Service, and the standards of behaviour expected of all civil servants in upholding these values.

- **Example:** Code of conducts include detailed guidelines on acceptance of gifts and specific behaviour regulating codes (class I officer shall not permit son or daughter to take employment with a company with which officer has official dealings’ or ‘acquiring immovable property with previous knowledge of prescribed authority’ and so on)

- In India, the current set of ethical norms are the Conduct Rules, contained in the Central Services (Conduct) Rules, 1964 and analogous rules applicable to members of the All India Services or employees of various State Governments. **Difference between Code of Ethics and Code of Conduct:** **Code of Ethics** It is a document that contains the core ethical values, principles and ideals of the organisation. **Code of Conduct** It is a document that contains the direction towards specific practices and behaviour that are followed or restricted under the organisation. It is publicly available (i.e. any one can access It is addressed to the employee only or to the it). civil servants. It is a set of principles which influence the It is a set of guidelines that influence judgement. It focuses more on values and principles. employee’s actions. It is focused on compliance and rules. Ethical standards generally are non-specific Conduct standards generally provide a fairly and are designed to provide a set of values or clear set of expectations about which actions decision-making . are required, acceptable or prohibited. Both the Codes are the standards that a group must adhere to as it helps any private as well as public institutions in regulating the day to day business efficiently. There is no Code of Ethics prescribed for civil servants in India although such codes exist in other countries. Therefore, there is a need for more generic norms to be laid down by way of accepted conduct for the civil servants as recommended by the Hota committee.

## Question 73 (Year: 2018 | Paper: GS IV) **Q2. (a) What is meant by public interest? What are the principles and procedures to be followed by civil servants in public interest? (150 Words, 10 Marks) Tag: Concept of public service; accountability and ethical governance; strengthening of ethical and moral values in governance.** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2018-gs4-q73-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Question** - **In Introduction, try to define public interest.** - **In Body, discuss the principles and** **procedures.** - **In Conclusion, try to write a way forward.** **Answer:** The term ‘public interest’ can be broadly defined as the set of actions or steps that result in the welfare of society and people in a greater way. Civil services or publicservicesplayacatalyticroleforthewelfareofthe people and for this public interest is the main criteria that influences decision making by public officials. As such policies and their implementation should be guided by public interest or public welfare. All government works have the overriding motive of public interest. Civil servants should be guided solely by public interest in their official decision making process. **Principles to be followed by the civil servants to** ## **uphold public interest** - **Compliance with Law:** Civil servant shall execute their official duties in compliance with the Constitution and laws. While performing their operations, civil servants shall act exclusively in the public interest.

- **Ethical Conduct:** The measures and steps taken by the public officials must be within the borders of ethical conduct. These values are the guiding principles that ensure social and public welfare most of the time.

- **Principles of Socialism:** For the welfare of the citizens of India, who are still underprivileged, needseverypossiblesupportfromthegovernment to excel. The civil servants or the public officials need to keep in mind the principle of socialism while formulating and implementing welfare policies and schemes.

- **Equal Treatment:** Civil servants shall ensure equal treatment of the citizens and the legal entities when performing official duties.

- **Good Public Relations:** While communicating with citizens and other legal entities, the civil servant shall act in a manner that establishes of relations of mutual confidence and cooperation between these entities and the administration.

- **Other:** Civil servant shall perform their activities in the most conscientious, direct, efficient, timely and methodical manner in the best interest of the citizens and the other entities in realising their rights, duties and interests.

## **Procedures to followed** - **Rules and Regulations:** Every public servant is expected to follow certain rules that govern their steps. These rules are framed in such a way that they help in achieving greater good of people if taken up with right intent.

- **Code of Conduct:** Officials are required to manage their conduct in public during most of the times. They are expected to be in check regardless of aggressive action by the public. Public service is for the public welfare. Policy formulation and implementation should be guided by public interest or public welfare. If a policy that is being formulated is perceived by the civil servants to be against public interest, it is their responsibility to convince the political executive about the adverse impacts of such a policy. But, if the political executive does not heed to such an advice, there is little that the civil servants can do other than putting their views clearly on record. Therefore, sometimes public interest cannot be followed due to certain conditions. These must be taken into consideration while designing rules and procedures. Besides this, all government works have the overriding motive of public interest. A civil servant should be guided solely by public interest in their official decision making.
