## Question 133(c) (Year: 2013 | Paper: GS IV) **(c) “Icounthimbraverwhoovercomeshisdesires than him who overcomes his enemies.”— Aristotle. (150 words, 10 marks) Tag: Contributions of moral thinkers and philosophers from India and world.** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2013-gs4-q133-unacademy**

## **Decoding the Question** - **In Introduction, try to give the context** **behind the given quotes/statements.** - **In Body, elaborate more on the given quotes** **and its relevance in the contemporary era.** - **In Conclusion, try to provide suggestions** **and conclude with the above points.** ## **Answers**
** (a) **“There is enough on this earth for every one’s need but for no one’s greed.” This most often quoted phrase by Mahatma Gandhi depicts his concern for nature and environment. Much before the convening of any of the international conferences like the Stockholm Conference of 1972 or the Rio Earth Summit of 1992 and almost a hundred years ago,** Mahatma Gandhi** **had voiced his concern for the environment. The** **concern was evident in his speeches, writings and his** **messages to the workers.** ## **Concepts of ‘Need’ and ‘Greed’** - Mahatma Gandhi’s approach to **nature revolves** **around the concepts of ‘need’ and ‘greed’** therefore, the bounties of nature can be used **only** **according to the needs.** - **Relevance in today’s context:** concept of need and greed leads to **grassroots efforts based** **on meeting the needs of all people on a** **sustainable basis.** - **In effort to reconcile the objectives of** **peace, environment protection and justice** based on satisfaction of needs of all people, the concept of Need and greed can be very useful, particularly as these are supported by his impressive record of living a life based on these principles and leading several struggles and campaigns based on these.

- MahatmaGandhiwasverymuchconcernedabout the sharing of resources by all. As he said that it is the fundamental law of nature, without exception that nature produces enough for our wants from day-to-long, and if only everybody took enough for himself and nothing more, there would be no pauperism in this world.

 - He forewarned humanity that, ‘a time is coming when those, who are in the mad rush today of multiplying their wants, vainly thinking that they add to the real substance, real knowledge of the world will retrace their steps and repent for their deeds.

 - **Relevance in today’s context:** In today’s scenario, **the ‘must-have, must-buy’** economy is eating into the planet’s resources like never before, something Mahatma Gandhi **foresaw** **three-quarters of a century ago. He also** **warned of the dangers of other countries** **taking to western industrialism.** - Mahatma Gandhi has been acknowledged as the ‘patron saint’ of the Indian environmental movement. Environmental activists have relied heavily on Gandhian thought of non-violent protest or satyagraha and have drawn heavily on Gandhian philosophy against heavy industry which may displace or crush the poor and downtrodden.

 - The Chipko movement (Chandi Prasad Bhatt and Sunder Lal Bahuguna), Baba Amte and Medha Patkar (Narmada Bachao Andolan) all derived inspiration from Gandhi.

- Mahatma Gandhi’s views on the environment consist of moral, spiritual and non-violent dimensions. India’s approach **on climate change** **mitigation is guided by Gandhian principles** and provisions of UNFCCC and Paris Agreement, particularly the principles of Equity and Common but Differentiated Responsibilities and Respective Capability (CBDR-RC). The **Gandhian philosophy and approach** give plenty of opportunities to overcome the environmental crisis. A frugal living and Spartan life may not be feasible for the present generation but a consideration with rationality and sensibility towards translating these ideas into practice would help in saving the planet Earth from the impending disasters. Gandhi belonged to that school of thought where **remedy was preferred** **to cure.** He worked out a worthwhile alternative for man’speaceful,purposefulandhappyexistence.Thisis what made him an environmentalist with a difference. **(b)** Abraham Lincoln is revered as one the greatest presidents that the United States has had, and much of his prestige can be attributed to his ability to reunite the country and lead the Union to victory in the Civil War. The quote given by Abraham Lincoln emphasises on power and its effect on man. People with personal power often change the course of other people’s lives. Depending upon the motives, it may be for the better or lead them down a dark or sinister path.

## **Power and its reflection on Man’s Character** - It can be said that power intrinsically is neutral, neither good nor bad. Once possessed it can be used to significantly advance the common good through the supportive actions of others. It reflects one’s will to the outside world through influencing other people.

- Unlikeadversity,powercanbeadeceptiveelevation ofthehumanego.Trickily,powerelevatesaperson above the realm of our true human nature, and most times **it sets us on a self-destructive battle** **of self-importance. For example, Hitler from** **Germany, and his ethnic cleanse demonstrated** **the worst of power.** - **Relevance in contemporary era:** Even though Abraham Lincoln said it over a century ago, **it is** **no less true today than it was when he first made** **the statement.** Not surprisingly, the authenticity of the statement is found in several spheres of human relationships. It is found **with the man entrusted** **with the duty to serve as leader over his fellow** **countrymen, who turns a dictator overnight.** For example, **Edi Amin,** the former president of Uganda promised for development and change but once he was in the power his era was marked by genocide, murder of opposition leaders, torture etc.

 - One major downside of power is, the more power one has, the less one is able to identify with others, implying less empathy. The powerful may become attuned to other powerful people but ignore their impact on the less powerful.

- **Powercangiveacapacitytoempathizewhichisfar** more important than **one’s class background.** For example, Winston Churchill was born in a palace, went to one of the top schools in the country, but he could speak directly to the needs of what he called the **cottage home.** When commanding in the trenches of the Great War, he put his earlier campaigning experience to **good use in always** **trying to ensure the men had their creature** **comforts,** a good postal service to connect them with their families. As it is said, **“With great power comes great** **responsibility”,** it can be attributed to a host of historically significant events and people, including Voltaire, Winston Churchill, President Roosevelt, Hitler, Napoleon. Some have used the power to change the fate of their country by making them superpower and some have led to complete destruction. **(c)** According to the given quote by Aristotle, the **greatestenemyformanisdesireasitsparksinitswake** attendant ills. **For instance, when desire is thwarted, it** **gives rise to anger and this upsets the balance in one’s** **nature.** It tempts humans to want for more and more to the extent of forgetting Him.

## **Biggest enemy for Man** - Holy scriptures like Gita and Buddhism also say that desire is the biggest enemy for man.

 - According to Gita, never ending desires is the main enemy of the human being. One has to defeat and conquer this enemy.

 - Desires along with other enemies such as anger, greed, pride or ego, attachment or delusion and jealousy attack the mind and try todistractthemindawayfromcontemplation. Thus, the enemy called Desire is internal and the war is also internal.

- According to Buddhism, **Desire and wrath** that springfromthenaturalpropertyofpassionhavean insatiable appetite for sensual pleasure and are the most sinful desire and wrath are the complements of attachment and repugnance.

 - Human beings are subject to desires and cravings, but even if they are able to satisfy thesedesires,thesatisfactionisonlytemporary. Pleasure does not last; or if it does, it becomes monotonous.

- **Relevance in Contemporary world: The desire** **of man** from technological advancement to conquer the world led to industrial revolution, Industrialisation and setting up colonies on others territory.

 - Repercussionsofthedesiregivenbirthtomany ‘Isms’likefactionindevelopedanddeveloping countries, Imperialism, Racism, Separatism and many more. Their repercussions can be seen on climate and environment too. Aristotle accepts that desire cannot be attributed for all purposive movement towards a goal. knowledge along with reason and desire work together to determine what is a good object of desire. In this regard, the Eightfold Path in Buddhist literature suggests to overcomethedesire.TheMiddlepathgivenbyBuddha avoids extremity in life and overcomes the extremities of desire.

## Question 138 (Year: 2024 | Paper: GS IV) **Q9.With multipronged strategy of the Central and State Governments specially in the last few years, the naxalite problem has been resolved to a large extent in the affected states of the country. However, there are a few pockets in certain states where naxalite problem still persists mainly due to involvement of foreign countries. Rohit is posted as SP (Special Operations) for the last one year, in one of the districts which is still affected by the naxalite problem. The district administration has taken a lot of developmental works in the recent past in the naxalite affected areas to win the hearts and minds of the people. Over a period of time, Rohit has established an excellent intelligence network to get the real time information regarding the movement of naxalite cadre. To instill confidence in the public and have moral ascendancy over the naxalites, a number of cordons and search operations are being conducted by the police. Rohit who himself was leading one of the contingents got a message though his intelligence source that about ten hard core naxalites were hiding in a particular village with sophisticated weapons. Without wasting any time, Rohit reached the target village with his team and laid out a foolproof cordon and started carrying out a systematic search. During the search, his team managed to overpower all the naxalites along with their automatic weapons. Avoiding civilian harm is crucial to maintaining public trust and ensuring that the security forces are seen as **protectors of the** **people, not oppressors.** - **True Protectors Don’t Use Violence:** Rohit can remind the tribal women that true saviors and protectors do not resort to violence or wield automatic weapons. - **Assign female police personnel** to engage with the women protesters, ensuring **cultural sensitivity** and avoiding unnecessary violence.

- Temporarilyredirectwaterresourcestoagriculture, prioritizing farmers’ needs during the crisis.

- **Empathy and Responsibility:** Responds to the farmers’ immediate concerns and acknowledges their dependence on agriculture for survival.

-

## **Pros** - Reduces the immediate discontent among farmers, placating the protests.

 - Supports local agriculture and helps farmers avoid crop failure.

## **y Cons** - Industrial operations may suffer, potentially leading to layoffs, reduced economic output, and unemployment.

- Implement community-based water-saving measures for farmers and promote the use of drip irrigated or rain-fed crops.

- Offer employment schemes in industries for those farmers who are heavily dependent on waterintensive crops.

## **y Ethical Considerations** - **Economic Stability:** Prioritizes industrial activity, which is crucial for providing employment and maintaining the district’s economic health.

 - **Pragmatism:** Acknowledges the reality that closing industries may lead to widespread unemployment, which could trigger greater social unrest.

## **y Pros** - Protects the local economy by sustaining industrial operations.

 - Reduces the risk of large-scale layoffs, preventing economic hardship for industrial workers.

## **y Cons** - Farmers may feel neglected and betrayed, worsening protests and potentially escalating the situation.

 - Prioritizing industries over agriculture may seem unjust, harming the administration’s credibility and moral standing. **Option 3: Apply Uniform Water Conservation Rules** **to Both Farmers and Industries** - Implement uniform restrictions on groundwater usage, applying the same rules to both farmers and industries.

- Directindustriestoadoptwater-savingtechnology, recycling, and rainwater harvesting techniques.

- Encourage farmers to shift to less water-intensive crops and provide government incentives for this transition.

- Set up a transparent mechanism to monitor water usage by both sectors.

## **y Ethical Considerations** - **Public Interest:** This option prioritizes the larger public interest by conserving water resources for drinking and essential use.

 - **Equity:** Treats both farmers and industries equally, ensuring no one sector is unfairly advantaged.

 - **Justice:** Creates a sense of fairness, reducing farmers’ perception of discrimination and preventing social unrest.

## **y Pros** - Ensures sustainable water usage and protects long-term water security.

 - Upholds the principles of fairness and transparency.

 - Reduces potential for corruption by monitoring all water users.

## **y Cons** - Mayresultineconomiclossesforbothfarmers and industries due to reduced water access.

 - Could lead to a confrontation with industries, which may resist regulatory measures. **(b) Suitable actions in view of mutually compatible** ## **interests of Stakeholders** - **Transparency in Water Allocation:** Make water usage and allocation data publicly available to prevent allegations of corruption and ensure transparency.

 - Builds trust among farmers, reduces the perceptionofbias,andpromotesaccountability in water management.

- **Encouraging** **Water-Saving** **Technologies:** Promote drip irrigation for farmers and recycled water usage for industries, providing subsidies or loans to implement these technologies.

 - Reducesthepressureongroundwaterandriver water sources while maintaining agricultural productivity and industrial operations.

- **Equitable Water Distribution and Compensation:** Implement an emergency compensation scheme for farmers who face crop losses due to water restrictionsandprovideemploymentopportunities in government programs (e.g., MGNREGA) to offset their income loss.

 - Balances the interests of the farmers without exacerbating the water crisis, reducing the likelihood of protests.

- **Creation of a Water Sharing Agreement:** Set up a committee with representatives from the farming community, industry, and administration to draft a water-sharing plan.

 - Ensures that each stakeholder understands the water crisis and agrees to a fair allocation system based on priority needs.

- **Conduct Regular Audits of Water Usage:** Implement regular audits of water usage for both farmers and industries to ensure compliance with regulations and address any discrepancies, fostering accountability.

 - It will foster accountability and ensure compliance with regulations, thus addressing the concerns of all stakeholders **(c) Potential Administrative and Ethical Dilemmas** ## **for the District Collector** ## **Administrative Dilemmas** ## **y Balancing Competing Stakeholder Interests** - **Farmers’ Needs vs. Industrial Needs:** The farmers, a vulnerable group, need water for their livelihoods, while the industries provide employment and contribute to the economy. TheDChastodecidehowtoallocatewaterina waythatdoesn’tharmeithergroupexcessively.

 - **Resource Allocation:** In a water-scarce scenario, determining who should receive priority—agriculture, industries, or public consumption—is a critical administrative challenge.

## **y Enforcement of Regulations** - **Strict Enforcement vs. Flexibility:** The DC is tasked with ensuring compliance with water conservation measures. However, strict enforcement can escalate tensions with farmers,whereasbeinglenientmayexacerbate the water crisis.

 - **Sustainable Practices:** The challenge is to ensure that farmers adopt sustainable practices while preventing industries from over-extracting groundwater.

## **y Managing Public Perception and Protests** - **Farmer Agitation:** Farmers are agitated over perceived administrative bias towards industries. If the administration is seen as favoring one group over another, it may lead to protests, risking social unrest.

 - **Media Scrutiny and Political Pressure:** The DC may face pressure from political leaders, media, and local communities to act swiftly and justly. Balancing transparency with effective governance in the face of public scrutiny is a significant challenge.

## **y Unemployment Concerns** - Shutting down water-intensive industries, which employ a large number of workers, can lead to widespread unemployment and economic decline. This creates a dilemma where the economic well-being of workers must be weighed against water conservation.

## **Ethical dilemmas** ## **y Fairness and Equity** - **EnsuringFairDistribution:Thefarmersallege** that the administration is biased towards industries, which calls into question whether the water distribution is being done equitably. The DC faces the ethical challenge of ensuring that both farmers and industries receive their fair share of water, without undue favoritism.

 - **Social Justice:** Water is a common resource. Ethically, the DC must ensure that weaker sections (like small farmers) are not disproportionately harmed while powerful stakeholders (industries) benefit.

## **y Transparency and Integrity** - **Allegations of Corruption:** Farmers allege that the administration is corrupt and has been bribed by industries. Even if these allegations are baseless, the DC must maintain transparency in decision-making to uphold public trust.

 - **Integrity in Decision-Making:** The DC must be vigilant against any potential conflicts of interest or influence from powerful industrial groups that may compromise impartial governance.

## **y Environmental Ethics** - **Sustainability:Ethically,theDCmustprioritize** long-term environmental sustainability over short-term economic or political gains. Allowing excessive groundwater extraction canleadtoirreversibleenvironmentaldamage.

 - **Precautionary Principle:** In the face of a severe water crisis, taking preemptive action, even if unpopular, may be ethically necessary to prevent further harm to the ecosystem.

## **y Duty vs. Compassion** - While enforcing water conservation measures is the DC’s duty, being compassionate towards the farmers who depend on irrigation for survival poses an ethical dilemma. The DC must balance duty with empathy to find a solution that doesn’t ruin farmers’ livelihoods.

## **y Utilitarianism vs. Rights-Based Approach** - **Greatest Good vs. Individual Rights:** The DC facesadilemmabetweenautilitarianapproach (shutting down industries or limiting farmers’ water usage to save resources for the greater good)andarights-basedapproach(protecting the individual rights of farmers and industrial workers).

 - **EthicalBalance:Choosingbetweenprotecting** the livelihoods of a large number of factory workers and ensuring that farmers, who are the backbone of rural economies, get water for their crops is an ethical balancing act.

- **Long-Term Planning vs. Immediate Crisis** ## **Management** - **Ethical Leadership in Crisis:** The DC faces an ethical dilemma of whether to focus on short-term crisis management (mitigating water shortages) or long-term solutions like promoting water-efficient practices in both agriculture and industry.

 - **Public Good vs. Economic Development:** The DC must consider the ethical imperative of water conservation for the public good, even if it comes at the cost of economic growth in the short term.

## **Conclusion** - In this situation, the District Collector must prioritize water conservation, transparency, and effective dialogue with all stakeholders.

During a team meeting, some senior team members suggest some shortcut for expediting the clinical trials for the drug and for getting the requisite approvals. These include manipulating data to exclude some negative outcomes and selectively reporting positive results foregoing the process of informed consent and using compounds already patented by a rival company, rather than developing one’s own component. Dr. Srinivasan is not comfortable taking such shortcuts, at the same time he realises meeting the targets is impossible without using these means. (a) What would you do in such a situation? (b) Examine your options and consequences in the light of the ethical questions involved.** ### Answer

## Question 145(c) (Year: 2024 | Paper: GS IV) **(c) How can data ethics and drug ethics save humanity at large in such a scenario? (250 words, 20 Marks)** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2024-gs4-q145-unacademy**

## **Introduction** - In the rapidly evolving field of biotechnology, ethical decision-making is critical, particularly in high-stakes situations like the development of a new drug to combat a fast-spreading infectious disease.

- Dr. Srinivasan, a senior scientist, faces immense pressure to expedite the clinical trials of a potentially life-saving drug amidst increasing cases globally.

- His team suggests unethical shortcuts, manipulating trial data, bypassing informed consent, and infringing on intellectual property rights to speed up the process and gain a competitive market edge.

- Dr. Srinivasan must navigate this ethical dilemma, balancing the urgent need for a solution with the potential long-term consequences of compromising scientific integrity and patient safety.

## **Body**
** (a) What would you do in such a situation? **InDr.Srinivasan’sposition,Iwouldrejecttheproposed

##** shortcuts for several reasons **-** Integrity and Responsibility: **Upholding scientific integrity is crucial, unethical practices can harm patients and violate ethical guidelines.

-** Long-term Impact: **Short-term gains could lead to severe long-term consequences, including loss of public trust and legal repercussions.

-** Whistleblowing: **If pressured to act unethically, I would report the issue to higher management or ethics committees to ensure transparency.

-** Seeking Alternatives: **I would explore legitimate ways to expedite trials, such as applying for accelerated approval or emergency use authorizations.** (b) Examine your options and consequences in the** **light of the ethical questions involved.** ## **Ethical Questions Involved** - **Patient Safety and Welfare:** Manipulating data or bypassing informed consent violates the fundamental principle of do no harm. Patients should always be fully informed about the risks involved in any trial.

- **Integrity of Science:** Misrepresenting data undermines the integrity of scientific research. It could distort future research outcomes and cause mistrust in medical science.

- **Professional Integrity:** As a senior scientist, Dr. Srinivasan has an obligation to uphold ethical standards and act as a role model for his team. **Option 1: Follow Ethical Standards** ## **Pros** - **Patient Safety:** Ensures that all clinical trial participants receive safe and effective treatment. Prioritizing their well-being fosters trust in the research process.

- **Scientific Integrity:** Maintaining rigorous standards upholds the credibility of the research, contributing to valid and reliable results that can advance medical knowledge.

- **Regulatory Compliance:** Adhering to ethical guidelines avoids potential legal issues, ensuring compliance with regulations from bodies like the FDA or EMA, which can lead to smoother approval processes.

- **PublicTrust:Upholdinghighethicalstandardscan** enhance public confidence in the pharmaceutical industry, which is vital for future drug uptake and compliance.

- **Long-term Success:** Building a reputation for ethical practices can lead to sustained success for the company, attracting investors and talent who value integrity.

## **Cons** - **Time Constraints:** Following ethical guidelines can slow down the trial process, potentially delaying the release of the drug, which may be critical in a public health crisis.

- **Market Competition:** Competitors who take shortcuts may secure market dominance first, potentially diminishing the company’s market share and profitability.

- **Pressure from Management:** There may be significant pressure from upper management to expedite results, which can create tension and conflict within the team.

- **Resource Allocation:** Rigorous adherence to ethical standards may require more resources (time, personnel, funding), potentially straining the project’s budget and timeline. **Option 2: Take the Suggested Shortcuts** ## **Pros** - **Faster Results:** Shortcuts may expedite the clinical trialprocess,allowingthedrugtoreachthemarket more quickly, which could be critical during a public health crisis.

- **First-Mover Advantage:** Gaining early access to the market can lead to substantial financial returns and brand recognition, establishing the company as a leader in the field.

- **Immediate Financial Gains:** Quick market entry could result in higher immediate profits, benefiting stakeholders and potentially leading to more funding for future projects.

## **Cons** - **Patient Risk:** Manipulating data and skipping informed consent can lead to unsafe drugs being released, endangering patients’ lives and health.

- **Legal Repercussions:** Engaging in unethical practices could result in lawsuits, regulatory fines, and criminal charges, severely damaging the company’s reputation and financial standing.

- **Loss of Scientific Credibility:** Such actions can undermine the trust of the scientific community, leading to skepticism about future research from the company.

- **Long-Term Consequences:** If the drug fails in the market or causes harm, it can lead to long-term damage to the company’s brand, loss of consumer trust, and decreased market value.

- **Career Risk:** Dr. Srinivasan and involved team members risk their professional reputations and careers by participating in unethical practices, which could lead to loss of licenses or job opportunities. **Option 3: Raise Concerns and Whistleblow** ## **Pros** - **Upholding Ethics:** Whistleblowing reinforces the importance of ethical practices within the organization,potentiallyleadingtoculturalchange and better standards.

- **Protecting Patients:** By raising concerns, it helps safeguard patients from potential harm caused by unethical practices.

- **Encouraging Accountability:** This option fosters a culture of accountability within the organization, prompting others to prioritize ethical behavior.

## **Cons** - PersonalRisk:Whistleblowersoftenfacebacklash, including isolation from colleagues, damage to professional relationships, and potential job loss.

- **Corporate Retaliation:** There is a risk of retaliation from management or peers, which can lead to a hostile work environment or even legal action against the whistleblower.

- **Uncertain Outcomes:** Raising concerns does not guarantee that action will be taken, and the situation may remain unresolved, leaving ethical violations unaddressed.

- **Emotional Toll:** The stress of whistleblowing can take a significant emotional toll on individuals, leading to anxiety or career-related dissatisfaction. **(c) How can data ethics and drug ethics save** **humanity at large in such a scenario?** **Data Ethics:** Data ethics refers to the principles and guidelines that govern the collection, usage, management,andsharingofdata,especiallyconcerning privacy, consent, transparency, and accountability. In the context of drug development and clinical trials, data ethics ensures that all data generated during the research process is handled responsibly and ethically.

- **Transparency:** Ensuring that data is accurate, complete, and transparent allows the scientific community and regulatory bodies to make informed decisions. This helps prevent the release of ineffective or harmful drugs into the market.

- **Accountability:** Ethical handling of data ensures that scientists are accountable for their findings. When data manipulation is avoided, there is a clear trail of evidence supporting or refuting the efficacy of a drug.

- **Trust in Science:** When the public knows that data is handled ethically, it fosters trust in scientific institutions, drug approvals, and the broader healthcare system. This is critical during pandemics or public health emergencies. **Drug Ethics:** Drug ethics refers to the moral principles and standards guiding the development, testing, approval, and marketing of pharmaceuticals. It encompasses the responsibilities of pharmaceutical companies,researchers,andhealthcareprofessionalsto prioritize patient welfare, safety, and rights throughout the drug development process.

- **Patient Rights:** Drug ethics protect patient rights, ensuring that they are not treated as mere subjects but are fully informed about risks and have provided their consent.

- **Safety and Efficacy:** Ethical trials ensure that drugs are thoroughly tested for both safety and efficacy, reducing the risk of adverse effects. This is especially critical in a rapidly spreading disease where widespread use of an ineffective or harmful drug could lead to a public health disaster.

- **Sustainability of Medical Research:** Upholding drug ethics encourages sustainable progress in medical research, where breakthroughs are based on rigorous testing and innovation, not on cutting corners.

## **Conclusion** - In this scenario, taking shortcuts might seem like a tempting solution to meet market demands, but the ethical, legal, and humanitarian consequences would far outweigh any potential benefits.

- Upholdingdataanddrugethicsnotonlysafeguards individual patients but also helps protect public trust in science and ensures that the solutions provided to global health crises are safe, effective, and sustainable. **2** **2023** ---

3. **EqualRemunerationAct,1976–Ensuresequalpay** for equal work, prohibiting wage discrimination based on gender.

## Question 159(d) (Year: 2022 | Paper: GS IV) **(d) What are the ethical dilemmas being faced by Ramesh? (e) What policy measures would you suggest to combat the menace of infiltration of illegal migrants from the neighbouring country? (250 words, 20 Marks)** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2022-gs4-q159-unacademy**

## **Answer** ## **(a) Options available to Ramesh** 1. Withdraw the report as instructed by the Additional Home Secretary.

2. Refuse to withdraw the report and face the potential consequences.

3. Approach higher authorities or other stakeholders about the issue.

4. Leak the report to the press or an investigative journalism outlet. **(b) Option Ramesh should adopt:** Ramesh should refuse to withdraw the report. It is essential to maintain the integrity of his office and fulfillhisresponsibilitiestothepublic.Ifheexperiences retaliation for doing so, he should appeal to higher authorities or even take legal action if necessary.

## **(c) Evaluation of options** - **Withdraw the report:** This would maintain Ramesh’s professional standing and ensure his promotion. However, it would compromise his integrity, allow illegal activities to continue, and potentially harm the state’s security and societal stability.

- **Refuse to withdraw the report:** This would uphold Ramesh’s integrity and commitment to his duties. However,itmayjeopardizehisjob,andpromotion, and could potentially strain his relationship with superiors.

- **Approach higher authorities or stakeholders:** This might lead to a thorough investigation and possible correction of the problem, maintaining Ramesh’s integrity. However, it may still risk Ramesh’s professional standing and relationships if those authorities also resist addressing the issue.

- **Leak the report to the media:** This could force the issue into public discussion and lead to actions to address the problem. However, it would risk Ramesh’s job and potentially put him in legal jeopardy. It could also be seen as a breach of professional protocol.

## **(d) Ethical dilemmas faced by Ramesh** - Personal vs Professional: Ramesh is torn between his professional duty to uphold justice and law and the personal needs of his ailing mother and children’s education.

- Loyalty vs Integrity: His loyalty to his superiors and the department is at odds with his personal integrity and his duty to the public. **(e) Policy measures to combat the menace of** ## **infiltration of illegal migrants** - **Strengthen border security: Invest** in better surveillance technology, more personnel, and improved training to prevent infiltration.

- **Increase penalties for corrupt practices:** Harsher punishments for officials who facilitate illegal immigration might deter such behavior.

- Develop a robust identification system: Improve the process of identification to prevent forgery of documents.

- **Increase international cooperation:** Work closely with the neighboring countries to better manage the cross-border movement of people.

- **Address root causes:** Support economic development and stability in the neighboring countriestoreducethepushfactorsleadingpeople to migrate illegally.

- **Strengthen legal immigration pathways:** Providing legal channels for those seeking to immigrate can help control the flow of people and reduce the demand for illegal pathways.

Ashok who was the Investigative Journalist working with leading TV channel, suo moto started investigating the case. Within one month, Ashok got breakthrough by interacting with local people, stone mining mafia and government officials. He prepared his investigative story and presented to the CMD of the TV channel. He exposed in his investigative report the complete nexus of stone mafia working with blessing of corrupt police and civil officials and politicians. The politician who was involved in the mafia was no one else but local MLA who was considered to be very close to the Chief Minister. After going through the investigative report, the CMD advised Ashok to drop the idea of making the story public through electronic media. He informed that the local MLA was not only the relative of the owner of the TV channel but also had unofficially 20 percent share in the channel. The CMD further informed Ashok that his further promotion and hike in pay will be taken care of in addition the soft loan of 10 lakhs which he has taken from the TV channel for his son’s chronic disease will be suitably adjusted if he hands over the investigative report to him. (a) WhataretheoptionsavailablewithAshoktocope up with the situation? (b) Critically evaluate/examine each of the options identified by Ashok. (c) What are the ethical dilemmas being faced by Ashok's** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2022-gs4-q160-unacademy**

## Question 161(d) (Year: 2022 | Paper: GS IV) **(d) Which of the options, do you think, would be the most appropriate for Ashok to adopt and why? (e) Intheabovescenario,whattypeoftrainingwould you suggest for police officers posted to such districts where stone mining illegal activities are rampant? (250 words, 20 Marks)** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2022-gs4-q161-unacademy**

## **Answer** ## **(a) Options available to Ashok** 1. Comply with the CMD’s advice, hand over the investigativereport,andnotexposethecorruption.

2. Refuse the CMD’s advice, and make the investigative report public through a different media outlet or through social media.

3. Submit the evidence and findings of the investigation to law enforcement agencies or anticorruption bodies.

4. Confront the involved politician and try to persuade him to end the illegal mining activities.

## **(b) Evaluation of the options** - **Comply with the CMD’s advice:** This would ensure Ashok’s job security, promotion, and financial relief concerning his son’s medical loan. However, it would compromise his professional integrity, contribute to the corruption issue, and fail to address the environmental and societal harm caused by illegal mining.

- **Refuse the CMD’s advice, and make the report** **public through a different outlet:** This action would uphold Ashok’s journalistic integrity and could force authorities to address the issue. However, it would risk his job and financial stability, and potentially lead to retaliation from powerful individuals involved in the corruption.

- **Submit the evidence to law enforcement or anti-** **corruption bodies:** This would ensure that the evidence is given to the authorities and potentially initiate an official investigation. However, it might not guarantee any immediate action, particularly if the corruption is deeply entrenched within the system.

- **Confront the involved politician:** This could potentially lead to a resolution if the politician responds positively. However, it is a risky strategy and could lead to retaliation.

## **(c) Ethical dilemmas faced by Ashok** - **Personal vs Professional:** Ashok is torn between his professional duty as a journalist to report the truth, and his personal need to maintain his job, promotion, and financial stability for his son’s medical treatment.

- **Integrity vs Loyalty:** Ashok’s commitment to integrity and truth is in conflict with his loyalty to his employer and the channel he works for. **(d) The most appropriate option:** The most appropriate option would be for Ashok to refuse the CMD’s advice and submit his evidence and findings to law enforcement agencies or anticorruption bodies. As a journalist, his first duty is to the truth. While it might risk his job and personal finances, he would be upholding his professional integrity. Furthermore, by involving law enforcement or anti-corruption bodies, he ensures that the issue is brought to the attention of those who can take formal action. **(e) Suggested training for police officers in such** ## **districts** - **Ethical Training:** Officers should be educated about the importance of integrity and ethical behavior in law enforcement. They should be trained to resist bribes and to report corruption.

- **Specific Illegal Mining Training:** Officers should receive training on the environmental, societal, and legal aspects of illegal mining. They should understand how to identify and investigate such activities.

- **Community Interaction:** Officers should be trained in community policing techniques, learning to build trust and rapport with local residents who can provide information on illegal activities.

- **Self-Defense and Emergency Response:** Given the violence that can be associated with such illegal activities, officers should receive robust training in self-defense and emergency response.

- **Whistleblower Protection:** Officers should be educated about their rights and protections as whistleblowers, to encourage reporting of corruption within the police force.

## Question 163(d) (Year: 2022 | Paper: GS IV) **(d) What are the ethical dilemmas being faced by you? (e) What can be the consequences of overlooking the observations raised by the Inspecting Team? (250 words, 20 Marks)** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2022-gs4-q163-unacademy**

## **Answer** ## **Stakeholders involved in the given case** - MBA Graduate (Employee): The MBA graduate, who secured the job in the Inspection Section, is a crucial stakeholder. Their interests include job security, professional integrity, and ethical responsibility towards their role in the company.

- Top Management: The top management of the leading shoe company holds significant power and authority. Their interests lie in maximizing profits, minimizinglosses,andmaintainingthecompany’s reputation.

- Inspecting Team Members: The other members of theInspectingTeamarestakeholderswithinterests injobsecurityandconformitytothemanagement’s directives. Their decisions may be influenced by the company’s culture, job dependency, and fear of potential consequences.

- Customers: The company’s customers are essential stakeholders with interests in receiving highquality products. Their satisfaction and trust in the company’s products impact its reputation and long-term success.

- Company Shareholders: Shareholders invest in the company with the expectation of returns on their investment. They are concerned about the company’s financial performance and long-term profitability.

- Dependent Family Members (Elderly Parents and Spouse): The employee’s dependent family members are stakeholders with interests in their well-being, financial stability, and job security.

- Domestic and International Markets: The markets where the company sells its products are stakeholders with interests in the quality and reputation of the brand. The company’s image can significantly impact its market share and growth opportunities.

- Competitors: Competing shoe companies are stakeholderswithinterestsinthemarketdynamics, potential market share gains, and the reputation of their own brands compared to the leading shoe company.

- Regulatory Authorities: Government agencies and regulatory bodies may have an interest in ensuring compliance with quality standards and consumer protection laws. **(a) Options available to you as a member of the** ## **Inspecting Team** - Comply with the Top Management: Clear the consignmentforthedomesticmarketasinstructed bythetopmanagement,despitetheglaringdefects, to avoid potential termination of your services.

- Stand Firm on Ethical Grounds: Refuse to clear the consignment, despite the consequences threatened by the top management, and maintain your integrity and professional ethics. **(b) Critical evaluation of each option:** **Comply with the Top Management:** Pros: Following the top management’s directive may protect your job and financial stability in the short term. Cons: Clearing a substandard product can harm the company’s reputation and jeopardize long-term businessprospects.Itmayalsoleadtoethicaldilemmas and personal guilt. **Stand Firm on Ethical Grounds:** Pros: Upholding ethical standards can build trust, integrity, and credibility, not only for you but also for the company. It may lead to long-term benefits and a positive impact on the company’s reputation. Cons: Standing against the top management’s decision may result in termination of your job and financial uncertainty. It could also lead to professional repercussions and strained relationships within the organisation. **(c) Option to adopt and why:** Giventhesituation,themostappropriateoptionwould be to stand firm on ethical grounds and refuse to clear the consignment despite the consequences threatened by the top management. This decision is based on the following reasons: Ethical Responsibility: As an employee, you have a moral duty to ensure that the products released by the company meet quality standards and do not compromise customer satisfaction or safety. Long-term Repercussions: Clearing a substandard consignment for the domestic market could have severe long-term consequences for the company’s image and reputation, resulting in potential loss of customers and business opportunities. Personal Integrity: Upholding ethical values and professional integrity is crucial for your self-respect and credibility in the industry. It will showcase your commitment to doing the right thing, even in challenging situations. **(d) Ethical dilemmas being faced by you:** Job Security vs. Ethical Integrity: The dilemma between safeguarding your job and adhering to ethical principles is a significant challenge. It puts your financial stability at odds with maintaining personal integrity. Professional Obligations vs. Organisational Pressure: The conflict arises between your duty to ensure quality inspection and the pressure exerted by the top management to overlook the defects for the sake of financial gains. **(e) Consequences of overlooking the observations** **raised by the Inspecting Team:** Reputation Damage: Clearing substandard products may lead to negative reviews and feedback from customers, causing significant damage to the company’s reputation. Legal Implications: Releasing defective products could expose the company to legal liabilities and potential lawsuits from customers or regulatory authorities. Lost Opportunities: Compromised product quality may result in lost opportunities to expand into international markets like Europe and Gulf countries, impacting the company’s growth prospects. Trust Erosion: Customers may lose trust in the company’s products, leading to decreased sales and diminished customer loyalty. In conclusion, as a member of the Inspecting Team, standing firm on ethical grounds and refusing to clear thesubstandardconsignmentisessentialtoprotectthe company’sreputation,maintainpersonalintegrity,and ensure long-term sustainability and growth. - Drivers’ Union: The workers’ union representing the drivers is a significant stakeholder. They are advocatingforfaircompensationandemployment opportunities for the deceased driver’s family and are responsible for the ongoing strike.

- City Transport Department Employees: Other employees of the City Transport Department are indirectly affected stakeholders. The strike may impact their work environment and cause disruptions to public transportation services.

- CityResidentsandCommuters:Thegeneralpublic and commuters who rely on the City Transport Department’s services are stakeholders. The ongoing strike may disrupt public transportation, affecting their daily lives.

- Local Authorities and Government: Local authorities and government officials may be stakeholders as the situation involves labour issues and public services. They may be involved in overseeing the resolution of the strike.

- Police Department: The police department is involved as they have registered an FIR related to thealtercationandaccidentinvolvingthedeceased bus driver.

- Hospital Staff: The hospital staff who attended to the injured parties are stakeholders, as they were involved in providing medical assistance after the altercation and accident. **(a) Options available to Rakesh to meet the above** **situation:** Recommend Full Extra Compensation: Rakesh can recommend to the City Transport Department management to grant the deceased driver’s family full extra compensation, similar to what is given to other drivers who died on duty. Advocate for Employment for a Family Member: Rakesh can also advocate for providing employment to one family member of the deceased driver as a compassionate gesture to support the family during their difficult time. Mediate between Management and Union: Rakesh can act as a mediator between the City Transport Department management and the workers’ union to findamiddlegroundandresolvethedeadlockthrough negotiations. Seek Legal Advice: Rakesh can seek legal advice to understand the legal obligations and implications regarding compensation and employment benefits for the deceased driver’s family. **(b) Critical examination of each option:** **Recommend Full Extra Compensation:** Pros:Granting full extracompensationto thedeceased driver’s family would demonstrate empathy and compassion, mitigating their financial hardships and grievances. Cons: The City Transport Department management may perceive this as setting a precedent, leading to similar demands from other drivers’ families in the future, potentially burdening the organisation financially. **Advocate for Employment for a Family Member:** Pros: Providing employment to one family member could offer long-term support and stability to the family. Cons: It may be challenging to find suitable job opportunities within the department, and this option may not address the immediate financial needs of the family. **Mediate between Management and Union:** Pros: Mediation may lead to a mutually acceptable solution, avoiding prolonged strike and its impact on public transportation. Cons: The management and union may have entrenched positions, making it difficult to find common ground and reach a consensus. **Seek Legal Advice:** Pros: Legal advice can provide clarity on the legal obligations and ensure compliance with relevant laws and regulations. Cons: Legal proceedings could be time-consuming and may not immediately resolve the emotional and agitatedstateofthedriver’sfamilyandunionmembers. **(c) Ethical dilemmas being faced by Rakesh:** Balancing Fairness and Precedent: Rakesh faces the dilemma of ensuring fairness to the deceased driver’s family while considering the potential precedent this decision may set for future cases. Managing Conflicting Interests: Rakesh must navigate the interests of the driver’s family, the workers’ union, and the City Transport Department management, which may not align with one another. Resolving the Strike: Rakesh faces the ethical dilemma of resolving the strike promptly to restore public transportation while addressing the legitimate concerns of the drivers’ union. **(d) Course of action Rakesh would adopt to diffuse** **the above situation:** Rakesh would adopt a multi-pronged approach to address the situation: ImmediateRelief:Rakeshwouldrecommendproviding full extra compensation to the deceased driver’s family as a gesture of support and compassion during their difficult time. Long-term Support: He would advocate for providing employment opportunities to one family member, takingintoaccounttheirqualificationsandpreferences. Mediation and Negotiation: Rakesh would actively mediate between the workers’ union and the City Transport Department management to find a middle groundandresolvethedeadlockthroughnegotiations. Legal Advice: Seeking legal advice would ensure that the recommended compensation and benefits align with the relevant laws and regulations. Communication and Empathy: Rakesh would communicatetransparentlywiththedriver’sfamilyand union members, showing empathy and understanding their concerns while explaining the constraints and challenges faced by the department. By adopting this approach, Rakesh aims to strike a balance between addressing the driver’s family’s immediateneeds,resolvingthestrike,andmaintaining the City Transport Department’s operational efficiency and financial sustainability.

- Colleagues: The colleagues who support the officer in their efforts to ensure environmental compliance and follow-up are also stakeholders, as they influence the officer’s decisions and actions.

- Local NGOs: Local Non-Governmental Organisations advocating for the closure of polluting units and environmental protection are stakeholders with interests in safeguarding the environment and public health.

Involve Local NGOs and Community: Engage with local NGOs and community members to collaborate on creating awareness about the importance of environmental compliance and the potential benefits it can bring to the region. **(b) Critical examination of the options listed:** Enforce Strict Compliance Measures: Pros: Upholding environmental compliance will protect the health of the local community and environment in the long run. Cons: The immediate closure of polluting industries may lead to unemployment and economic instability for the labourers and owners. Engage in Dialogue and Negotiation: Pros: Open communication and negotiation may lead to finding middle-ground solutions that address both environmental concerns and economic livelihoods. Cons: Some industrial unit owners may not be willing to cooperate, leading to delays in addressing pollution issues. Cons: It may take time to receive a response from higher authorities, and immediate action may be required to address the pressing pollution issues. Involve Local NGOs and Community: Pros: Involving local NGOs and community members can garner support for environmental compliance and create a collective understanding of its importance. Cons: There may be resistance from some stakeholders who prioritise economic interests over environmental concerns. **(c) Mechanism to ensure environmental compliance:** To ensure environmental compliance effectively, the following mechanisms can be suggested: Regular Monitoring and Inspections: Establish a regular monitoring and inspection system to assess industrial units’ compliance with environmental regulations. Strengthen Enforcement: Implement strict penalties for violations to deter industries from non-compliance and ensure that the law is enforced without bias. Public Awareness Campaigns: Conduct public awareness campaigns to educate the local community about the importance of environmental protection and the potential benefits it brings. IncentivesforSustainablePractices:Provideincentives and support for industrial units that adopt sustainable and eco-friendly practices to encourage compliance. **(d) Ethical dilemmas faced in exercising your** ** options: **Balancing Environmental Protection and Livelihoods: The ethical dilemma of finding a balance between enforcing environmental compliance to protect the environment and considering the economic impact on labourers and owners who rely on these industries for their livelihoods. Handling Threats and Hostile Response: The ethical dilemma of ensuring your own safety and wellbeing while upholding your responsibility to enforce environmentalcompliancedespitethreatsandhostility. Addressing Stakeholder Interests: The ethical dilemma of addressing the interests and concerns of various stakeholders,includingthelocalcommunity,industrial unit owners, and labour union, while striving for a resolution that benefits all parties. Dealing with Pressure and Influence: The ethical dilemma of maintaining independence and objectivity in decision-making while dealing with pressure from vested interests and local politicians who may attempt to influence the outcome. ## Question 170(b) (Year: 2021 | Paper: GS IV) **(b) Critically examine the options available with you as Vice Principal. What option will you adopt and why? (20 Marks, 250 Words) Tags: Case Study** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2021-gs4-q170-unacademy**

## **Answer** ## **Stakeholders** - Self as vice principal

- College management

- Senior lecturer

- Students Protesting

- Flying squad

## **Ethical issues involved** - Professional duty vs personal gains

- Order of management and image of college vs justice to other honest students

- Fund and affiliation for college vs voice of protesting students

## **Options** - **1st Option:** Follow management’s orders and reach an agreement by concealing the truth from the flying squad.

- **2nd Option:** Demonstrate to management your inability to deal with the flying squad.

- Except for the senior lecturer, all other college staff are honest, according to the flying squad.

- The student learns the importance of honesty in both school and life.

- Do justice to a large number of students, including those protesting outside the college.

## **Demerits** - It is possible that I will not be promoted to principal.

- Thecollege’simageandreputationmaybeharmed, funding may be halted, and affiliation may be terminated.

- Local politicians and businessmen may also harm me in all aspects of my life.

## **Justification for 3rd option** - As a vice principal, I chose the third option to maintain my professional integrity, which may jeopardise my current personal gains but will undoubtedly give me a sense of confidence and job satisfaction in the future.

- By doing so, I convey to management that the college’s image will not be tarnished, but that a proper precedent will be set in college, and that supporting such senior lecturers will further degrade the college in the future.

- I also express that supporting such dishonest students will tarnish the image of the college in the future and will be unpopular with society and the nation at large.

- I would like to request that the flying squad not take such harsh action that destroys a student’s future, but rather that a light punishment be given so that their career prospects are not harmed, and they learn a lesson.

## Question 172(d) (Year: 2021 | Paper: GS IV) **(d) What can be the consequences of overlooking the observation raised by the inspecting team? (20 Marks, 250 Words) Tags: Case Study** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2021-gs4-q172-unacademy**

## **Answer**
** (a)** **Options available to me as the project manager are as** ## **under** - **Option 1:** I have the option of continuing with the work because my superior, the chief engineer, has overruled the inspection team’s findings.

- **Option 2:** I can conduct additional research into the issue and bring it to the attention of senior political leaders or the CM himself.

- **Option 3:** Because I believe the project’s quality is compromised and have expressed my concerns to my superiors, I can request my removal from the project to avoid liability in the event of a contingency. **(b)** **Ethical dilemma faced by the project manager are as** ## **under** - Personal interest vs. public interest: While moving forward with the project will guarantee a promotion, it may jeopardise the lives of many citizens.

- Ends vs. means: While moving forward with the project will meet the end goal of a timely inauguration and my promotion, it will degrade the purity of the means involved.

- Cost vs. quality of service.: While demolishing the three pillars will increase the project’s cost and time overrun, especially if approved by the chief engineer, continuing will compromise the qualityof-service delivery.

- Administrative ethics vs. work efficiency: While the project manager will be praised for his efficiency in completing the project on time, it will jeopardise his administrative ethics. **(c)** **Professional challenges likely to be faced by the** ## **manager are as under** - Pressure from superiors: The Chief Engineer exhorted the project manager to continue working at the same pace.

- Political pressure: The contractor is a minister’s relative, and the CM wants to open the project before the elections.

- Career advancement: Because the project manager’s career prospects are linked to the project’s completion.

- Possible liability: If the project fails and the bridge collapses, the project manager may face a departmental/judicial investigation.

- Corruption: Because the material used was of poor quality, the project manager must deal with corruption in his department. **The project manager can overcome these challenges** ## **by** - Presenting a detailed report on his assessment and sending a copy to the CM, concerned minister, chief engineer, chief secretary, and state vigilance officer.

- In the event of an anomaly, conducting an investigation into the quality of the material used and sending a show cause notice to the concerned parties.

- Involving a third party to conduct an independent survey on the corridor’s fitness/health. **(d)** **The consequences of overlooking the observations of** ## **the inspecting team could be** - The corridor could collapse, causing severe damage to citizens’ lives and property.

- The collapse will result in a higher cost and time overrun in the project, as the entire project will have to be restarted.

- Failure of the project will bring a bad name to both the department and the state’s political leadership.

- Ignoring the inspection team’s observations also means avoiding the issue of poor material quality in the project.

- It will also have the unintended consequence of encouraging nepotism in the administration of government projects.

**Question ID: 2021-gs4-q174-unacademy**

## Question 7 (Year: 2020 | Paper: GS IV) **Q7. Rajesh Kumar is a senior public servant, with a reputation of honesty and forthrightness, currently posted in the Finance Ministry as the Head of the Budget Division. His department is presently busy in organizing budgetary support to the states, four of which are due to polls within the financial year. This year’s annual budget had allotted Rs 8300 crores for the National Housing Scheme (NHS), a centrally sponsored social housing scheme for the weaker sections of society. Rs 775 crores have been drawn for the NHS till June. The Ministry of Commerce had long been pursuing a case for setting up a Special Economic Zone (SEZ) in a southern state to boost exports. After two years of detailed discussion between the centre and state, the Union Cabinet approved the project in August. Process was initiated to acquire the necessary land. Eighteen months ago a leading Public Sector Unit (PSU) had projected the need for setting up a large natural gas processing plant in a northern state for the regional gas grid. The land is already in possession of the PSU. The gas grid is an essential component of the national energy security strategy. After three rounds of global bidding the project was allotted to an MNC, M/S XYZ Hydrocarbons. The first tranche of payment totheMNCisscheduledtobemadeinDecember. The Finance Ministry was asked for a timely allocation of an additional Rs 6000 crores for these two developmental projects. It was decided to recommend re-appropriation of this entire amount from NHS allocation. The file was forwarded to the Budget Department for their comments and further processing. On studying the case file, Rajesh Kumar realized that this re- appropriation may cause inordinate delay in the execution of NHS, a project much publicized in the rallies of senior politicians. Correspondingly, non-availability of finances would cause financial loss in the SEZ and national embarrassment due to delayed payment in the international project. Rajesh Kumar discussed the matter with seniors. He was conveyed that this politically sensitive situation needs to be processed immediately. Rajesh Kumar realized that diversion of funds from the NHS could raise difficult questions for the government in the Parliament. Discuss the following with reference to this case: (a) Ethical issues involved in re-appropriation of funds from a welfare project to the development projects. Tag: Case Studies.** ### Answer **Answer:** **Case Summary:** Rajesh Kumar, Head of Budget Division in the Finance Ministry, Known for his honesty and forthrightness.

## **The main issues of concern before him are** - Budget allocated for the NHS scheme is Rs. 8300 cr of which 775 cr is drawn already in June, now left with Rs. 7525 cr.

- In August, the Union Cabinet approved a project to set up SEZ by the Ministry of Commerce to increase exports, and the process for land acquisition began.

- PSU to set up a natural gas processing plant after globalbiddingallotthe projecttoMNC,M/SXYZ, for which the first tranche of payment to MNC to be made in December.

- The Finance Ministry was asked for a timely allocation of an additional Rs. 6000 Cr for these two development projects. It was decided to recommend re-appropriation of this entire amount from allocation. Rajesh Kumar discussed the matter with his seniors. Hewasconveyedthatthispoliticallysensitivesituation needs to be processed immediately. Rajesh Kumar realized that diversion of funds from the NHS could raise difficult questions for the government in the Parliament.

## **Stakeholders in the case** - Beneficiaries of National Housing Scheme (NHS).

- Public Sector Unit for Natural gas processing plant

- MNC M/S XYZ Hydrocarbons

- Southern State where SEZ to be set up.

- Senior bureaucrats and Politicians of different Ministries.

- Society at large. **Ethical Issues Involved in re-appropriation of funds** ## **from a welfare project to the development projects** - **Business Environment Vs Budget guidelines:** Based on budget guidelines, funds are allocated on different schemes. One may have to compromise with the budgetary provisions in order to maintain the business environment favourable for setting up of SEZ or natural gas processing plants.

- **Public Accountability Vs International Ethics** **(Commitment):** This is again an ethical dilemma whether we should look for welfare projects or we should look for India’s international image or commitment made to foreign companies.

- **Infrastructure Development Project Vs Welfare** **Project:** Both Infrastructure development project andwelfareprojectarecrucialforthedevelopment of the country. Infrastructure development projects like, the SEZ and natural gas processing plant are developmental proiect, which has huge regional growth potential in terms of employment generation, economic growth of the regions, etc. Ontheotherhand,welfareprojectsarealsocrucial for their weaker section of the society. Therefore, it is also an ethical dilemma.

- **Rights based Approach Vs administrative** **discretion:** Though the Ministry of Finance has given administrative discretion to allocate 6000 cr rupees or go for reappropriation but again the ethical dilemma will arise when administrative discretion is used as it will be against the right of the people (having the right to have basic amenity of housing).

- **Policy dilemma to prioritize welfare or** **Development:** This is also an ethical issue or ethical dilemma as we need to prioritise between welfare scheme or development. This is a classic dilemma between welfare and Growth. It was believed that Development would lead to a trickle down effect on poor and weaker sections of society, which did not happen. Hence, there was a need to have seperate welfare program for the weaker section of society to ensure sustainable and inclusive growth. In the above case, there were issues associated with re-appropriation of funds meant for the NHS for the development of the Special Economic Zone (SEZ) in the Southern state for export promotion and PSU led National gas processing plant for a regional gas grid to ensure national energy security by an MNC. SEZ and the gas grid may be important from an economic growth perspective but often the interest of the poor gets ignored. NHS is an essential aspect of equity and meaning distribution of welfare benefits to every section of society. Thus, a balance between equity and economic growth must be the best way forward. **Given the need for proper utilization of public funds,** ** the options available to Rajesh Kumar are:** **Resigning from Job:** This will ensure an easier way out to the problem encountering Rajesh Kumar. But again, it would be akin to the escapist tendency where Rajesh Kumar could be seen as running away from responsibility. An honest and forthright public servant is expected to handle the situation to the best of their capabilities and take decisions as per their merit. Hence, this option would be least preferable. **Divert the Fund:** This will ensure that both SEZ projects and Natural Gas processing plants would have sufficient funds to initiate their process. However, after re-appropriation ordiversionoffundsdemanded,i.e.600cr.only1525cr wouldbeleftfortheNHS,whichiswhyitisinsufficient for its implementation. It was observed that poor and marginalised are often overlooked in the development process and this action would strengthen this view point. Hence, this option too is not preferable. **Do not Divert the Fund:** By not diverting the fund, the NHS would be implemented in the way it was planned and the poor would get their own housing accommodation. It also serves the government face in parliament. However, it will lead to default in payment to M/S XYZ for Natural gas processing plants that would det Country’s international reputation. Likewise, the SEZ Project land acquisition process would further get delayed. Hence, this option too is not preferable. **Balancing the Reappropriation of Fund:** Here, the budget reappropriation is done in a way that payment of the first tranche to M/S XYZ is made on time along with some funds for SEZ Project to ensure its kick start. However, the majority of funds that was allocated for the NHS scheme should be there with a clause of release of funds in a phased manner with progress of Housing Construction to ensure houses for need one built on time. In this case, the best course of action would be to balance the economic growth initiative under the SEZ and National Grid project with people’s rights and welfare. It could be done with the help of better resource and budget management during allocation based on outcome based budgeting principles. Hence, the 4th option would be the most appropriate option.

## Question 179 (Year: 2020 | Paper: GS IV) **Q8. The Chairman of Bharat Missiles Ltd (BML) was watching a program on TV wherein the Prime Minister was addressing the nation on the necessity of developing a self-reliant India. He subconsciously nodded in agreement and smiled tohimselfashementallyreviewedBML’sjourney in the past two decades. BML had admirably progressed from producing first generation anti- tank guided missiles (ATGMs) to designing and producing state of the art ATGM weapon systems that would be the envy of any army. He sighed in reconciliation with his assumptions that the government would probably not alter the status quo of a ban on export of military weaponry. To his surprise, the very next day he got a telephone call from the Director General, Ministry of Defence, asking him to discuss the modalities of increasing BML production of ATGMs as there is a probability of exporting the same to a friendly country. The Director General wanted the Chairman to discuss the details with his staff in Delhi next week. Two days later, at a press conference, the Defence Minister stated that he aims to double the current weapons export levels within five years. This would give an impetus to financing the development and manufacturing of indigenous weapons in the country. He also stated that all indigenous arms manufacturing nations have a very good record of international arms trade. As the Chairman of BML, what are your views on the following points? a. As an arms exporter of a responsible nation like India, what are the ethical issues involved in the arms trade? (250 Words, 20 Marks) Tag: Case study** ### Answer **Answer:** **Case Summary:** It is given in the case, the Chairman of BML who believed the country would not export its state of art Anti Tank Guided Missile (ATGM) weapons. Later he gets to know about the intention of the government to export ATGM to friendly countries and double the weapon export within the next 5 years. It impetus and boost to indeginous defence manufacturing and will lead to export of arms.
**Question ID: 2020-gs4-q179-unacademy**

## **Stakeholders Involved in the Case** - Chairman of BML and its employees

- The Government of India

- Foreign government

- Society at large

## **Ethical issues involved in the case** - As an arm exporter of a responsible nation like India, he must ensure that the arms exported should not be used for any unethical conduct or should not cause any destruction. This can be ensured by signing a formal agreement on the use of arms with the friendly nations (who are the importers).

- India has always been an ardent supporter of multilateral nuclear disarmament and nonproliferation efforts. So, exporting arms may increase arm proliferation in different parts of the world, which further may become a threat to internal peace and security..

- Exportof armsmightcreatesomekindofthreatof international peace and security if the arms reach the wrong hands or terrorist groups.

- He must also ensure that export of arms should not promote or trigger arm race.

- It also needs to be taken care that the country (who imported arms from India) should not use it against its own people as it is the principle of International ethics especially human rights.

## **their legitimate needs** - It should be at par with international regulation. No international arm trade could be conducted without following the international regulation.

## Question 180 (Year: 2020 | Paper: GS IV) **Q9. Rampura, a remote district inhabited by a tribal population, is marked by extreme backwardness and abject poverty. Agriculture is the mainstay of the local population, though it is subsistence due to the very small landholdings. There is insignificant industrial or mining activity. Even the targeted welfare programs have inadequately benefited the tribal population. In this restrictive scenario, the youth has begun to migrate to other states to supplement the family income. Plight of minor girls is that their parents are persuaded by labour contractors to send them to work in the Bt Cotton farms of a nearby state. The soft fingers of the minor girls are well suited for plucking the cotton. The inadequate living and working conditions in these farms have caused serious health issues for the minor girls. NGOs in the districts of domicile and the cotton farms appear to be compromised and have not effectively espoused the twin issues of child labour and development of the area. You are appointed as the District Collector of Rampura. Identify the ethical issues involved. Whichspecificstepswillyouinitiatetoameliorate the conditions of minor girls of your district and to improve the overall economic scenario in the district. (250 Words, 20 Marks) Case studies** ### Answer
**Question ID: 2020-gs4-q180-unacademy**

## **Tag**
** Answer:** **Case Summary:** Rampura is a remote district, backward with tribal population, having poverty and locals are associated with subsistence agriculture. Insignificant industries, inadequate targeted welfare programs leading to migration of youth including minor girls to work with poor working conditions that affect the health of minor girls. NGOs failed to deal with twin problems such as child labour and area development. I am the district collector of Rampura.

## **Stakeholder in the case** - I, appointed as a district collector in Rampura.

- Local population of Rampura.

- Labour contractor.

- Local Non Government Organizations (NGOs).

## **Ethical Issues involved in the case** - **Corruption:** Corruptionisanethicalissue.Itisthe result of erosion of values of the public servants. The practice of corruption is well visualised in this case as the district has insignificant industrial or mining activity and even the targeted welfare programs have inadequately benefited the tribal population. This is because the benefit has not reached the end people.

- **Neglect of Regional Development:** It is a serious ethical issue because, due to the backwardness of the district and lack of will of the public servant or the concerned authority, the development of the region (Rampura district) gets neglected. If the concerned authority works as per their administrative and official capacity, they could develop the region through proper utilisation of funds and proper implementation of schemes.

- **Child Labour:** Here in this case, child labour is an ethical issue because child labour is prohibited under the Child Labour (Prohibition and Regulation) Act of 1986.

- **Exploitation of Labour:** Labour are being exploited by employers or managers in various ways like, long working hours and forced overtime work with less wage. This practice is prevalent in most of the industries, especially in garment industries. This is also an ethical issue in this case as inadequate living and working conditions in these farms have caused serious health issues for the minor girls. **Steps that will taken to ameliorate the conditions of** ## **minor girls** - **Strictly Implement Child Labour Act:** The Child Labour (Prohibition and Regulation) Act of 1986 designates a child as a person who has not completed their 14th year of age. It aims to regulate the hours and the working conditions of child workers and to prohibit child workers from being employed in hazardous industries. Proper enforcement of and implementation of this Act is crucial to ameliorate the conditions of minor girls.

- **Alternative Livelihood Opportunity:** Need to provide alternative livelihood opportunity by implementing targeted government programs and government schemes like MGNREGA, Mudra Loan, etc., to support them economically.

- **Rehabilitation of Minor Girl Child:** Rehabilitation of minor girl child under the Ujjawala Yojana is crucial.

- **Education of Minor Girl Child:** To ameliorate the minor girl child, education is crucial.

- **Others:** Need to provide vocational and skill development training for minor girl children. They are also provided with nutrition and health care under the Poshan Abhiyan. **Steps that will taken to improve the overall economic** ## **scenario in the district** - **Promotion of Development Programs:** Being a district collector in Rampura, i will promote and implement all the state or centrally sponsored development programmes or schemes like Deen Dayal Upadhyaya Grameen Kaushalya Yojana, MGNREGA etc. This will help in improving the economic scenario in the district to a great extent.

- **Focus on Skill Development:** I will focus more on skill development of the locals under the Pradhan Mantri Kaushal Vikas Yojana (PMKVY) and also provide all the necessary administrative support to make them skilled. If the locals are skilled, they can get decent jobs with decent wages, that will definitelyhelpthemtohaveagoodandqualitylife.

- **Implementation of Tribal Welfare Schemes:** I will implement various state or centrally sponsored tribal welfare schemes (if they are not implemented) and also provide resources under the administrative capacity to make them forward.

- **Establishment and Promotion of Agro-based** **Small Scale Industries:** Establishment and promotion of agro-based small scale industries will play as a catalytic role in the improvement of the overall economic scenario and will also generate employment.

- **PromotionofTribalProducts:Promotionoftribal** products under TRIFED (Tribal Cooperative Marketing Development Federation of India). This will significantly increase the income of the locals and also improve the economic scenario of the district.

- **Regulation of NGO:** Needs to regulate NGOs and their funding based on their work. It is a very crucialstepthatneedstobetakenfortheadvantage of the deprived and poor locals.

- **Support from Legislatures:** I will try to gather support from local MLAs, MPs of the region to take developmental initiatives from MLA lad and MP lad fund. Thus, in this way, the problems of the Rampura district will be resolved and this will also ensure and promote faster, sustainable and inclusive growth.

## Question 181 (Year: 2020 | Paper: GS IV) **Q10. You are a municipal commissioner of a large city, having the reputation of a very honest and upright officer. Permission to build by my senior, who is well known to me and a good friend. - Society at large.

## **Ethical issues involved in the case** - **Crony capitalism:** An economic system characterized by close, mutually advantageous relationships between business leaders and government officials. They both work for their vested interest and support each other.
